- Before committing or pushing `rules.csv`, review every changed record against the exact publication base. Validate the selected CSV with Starsector's native loader, check Ink parity, pagination, spoken quotation marks, duplicate or malformed option labels, stray closing quotes or punctuation, unresolved tokens, and choice dispatch including shortcuts. Confirm that dialogue describes the code and assets actually included in that commit, and that the matching jar implements its commands and option handlers. A parsing/build pass alone is insufficient. - Validate dialogue CSV with the installed game's native loader through external `tools/RulesCsvValidation.java` in `build.ps1`, before compiling/packaging. PowerShell Import-Csv accepts malformed closing-quote suffixes that shift Starsector's quoted state for the rest of the file; its last-row error may be far from the real defect. Kleon's Ungaikyo briefing ends with one quoted spoken sentence and the normal CSV field terminator, without a second `."""` suffix. Keep existing prose, flags, choices, rewards and unrelated dialogue intact. Do not import obfuscated engine internals into production code; native parser inspection/invocation stays in the external verification tool.

## Starsector sprite orientation — recurring pitfall

- Do **not** assume that the top of a PNG will render toward the top of the combat map when a ship or station core has `facing = 0`.
- Starsector treats the top of conventional hull art as the hull's forward direction, while combat facing `0°` points east. An upright source sprite therefore appears rotated by 90° at `facing = 0`.
- For art authored with its forward edge at the top of the PNG: use `90°` to face north, `270°` to face south, `0°` to face east, and `180°` to face west.
- A `.ship` center is measured from the sprite's bottom-left, not its top-left. For an upright hull, map local engine coordinates to PNG pixels with `pixelX = centerX - localY` and `pixelY = height - centerY - localX`. Gautama's engine origins are authored against the painted rear silhouette of `chief_navigator_scylla_v2.png`, not the scaled vanilla Fabricator's empty-margin positions. All three Gautama skins inherit the base engine geometry; retain the fourteen engines and their angles, flare sizes, style, weapons, center, and other hull geometry. Check `tools/validate_gautama_engines.ps1`; do not infer engine placement from the older hardpoint validator's top-origin Y assumption.
- This has caused multiple Chief Navigator station and wall sprites to be laid out 90° wrong. Treat it as a recurring integration hazard, not an isolated mistake.
- Before tuning module spacing, arcs, collision bounds, or weapon offsets, first verify the rendered cardinal orientation in combat with one asymmetric module. Do not compensate for a 90° orientation error by moving or rotating every child slot.
- For a horizontal station-core layout whose module centers are already correct, rotate the `STATION_MODULE` slot angles rather than rotating the parent core, because changing the parent also rotates the attachment layout. GDI+ frame composition and Starsector child-module facing apply opposite signs to the visual curvature. If the parent-frame berth curves are `9, 6, 3, 0, -3, -6, -9` around a 180-degree visual baseline, use the inverse curvature around the insert's `270°` slot baseline: `261, 264, 267, 270, 273, 276, 279`. Reusing the frame's curvature sign produces per-module errors of approximately `-18, -12, -6, 0, +6, +12, +18` degrees; it is not a uniform offset.
## Modular ship and station design rules

- Treat every station or ship module as a separate ship attached to a controllable parent. Each module has its own armor grid, flux pool, ship system, hullmods, and combat state. Use this separation to create progressive destruction and local defenses rather than inflating one hull's global statistics.
- Avoid extreme armor (especially above roughly 2,000) because Starsector's armor calculation can make low-damage weapons functionally irrelevant. Avoid extreme flux/dissipation because percentage-based hard-flux dissipation can make a boss impossible to meaningfully pressure. Prefer several reasonably tuned destroyable modules.
- Modular defenses can easily become overtuned. Give the encounter explicit weaknesses: vulnerable gaps, a comparatively fragile parent, arcs that can be flanked, or modules whose loss materially reduces the boss. Players should feel the station weaken as sections are destroyed.
- Players cannot directly control ordinary ship modules. Keep enough meaningful weapons or systems on a player-controllable parent; otherwise piloting it becomes passive. This matters less for NPC-only FOB Ithaca structures, but remains a useful design check.
- Enemy AI counts modules as separate ships. A capital parent with five capital-class modules may be evaluated as six capital ships and discourage engagement. Unless a specific behavior requires otherwise, give attached modules a small hull size (generally frigate) and consider the station-PD-drone treatment for non-primary defensive plates so AI threat assessment remains sane.
- Hostile AI tends to aim at ship/module center points. Armor-module center offsets can manipulate targeting toward the parent's center and expose intentional gaps, but they distort some UI previews and should not be a default for weapon or functional modules. Prefer a correctly centered sprite plus `moduleAnchor` for ordinary placement; use center-offset targeting tricks only deliberately.
- `always_detaches` should be the normal destruction behavior so a killed section breaks away and opens the parent. `never_detaches` leaves a dead module as a large obstruction and can turn fights into slogs; use it only when the resulting obstruction has exploitable gaps and is intentionally part of the encounter.
- Every module requires an entry in `ship_data.csv`, its own `.ship`, and its own `.variant`. The parent needs the `SHIP_WITH_MODULES` hint. Module hulls should normally be `UNBOARDABLE` and `HIDE_IN_CODEX`; use `DO_NOT_SHOW_MODULES_IN_FLEET_LIST` when they must also disappear from fleet/refit selectors. A module with no OP is effectively unselectable even without a tag.
- Useful module hints/tags: `UNDER_PARENT` renders the module below its parent; `module_hull_bar_only` hides module flux UI while retaining its hull bar; `module_no_status_bar` hides all mouseover/lock-on status and prevents selection; `module_unselectable` blocks refit customization; pair `module_refit_bright` with it when the module should remain normally lit in refit.
- Mount modules on `STATION_MODULE` slots. Placement is the relationship between the slot location and the child sprite's center, or preferably the child's `moduleAnchor` when the visual attachment point differs from its image center. Keep ordinary module sprite centers at the image center so previews remain correct.
- Variant module order is render order: modules later/lower in the parent `.variant` render above earlier modules. Parent-hull weapons render above ordinary ship modules and below fighter modules. Use `UNDER_PARENT` and variant order intentionally instead of compensating with duplicate background sprites.
- Useful module hullmods: `axialrotation` rotates about the module axis; `anchorrotation` rotates about its attachment anchor; `shield_always_on` can combine with the latter for rotating shield emitters; `reduced_explosion` protects the parent from module death blasts; `vastbulk` keeps a parent alive until its modules die; `do_not_fire_through` discourages other modules firing through it; `shared_flux_sink` redistributes its contribution across surviving modules.
- Officer skills affect a parent and its attached modules, while hullmods remain per-module. Account for both when balancing the station rather than evaluating the raw CSV stats in isolation.

## Current implementation invariants

- The Hegemony liaison Irina Sokolova is retired. Never create or offer her Ithaca contact or dialogue, and Voss leaves no liaison behind after the command conference. Keep historical person IDs and markers only to exclude serialized contacts from the directory without deleting save objects. Voss's expedition, confrontation, requisitions, and patrols remain active.

- Treat authored campaign topology as one-time generated, serialized state. A normal game load, Intel/chart open, or interaction lookup must never delete, replace, recreate, relocate, rename, or rewire an existing system, jump point, gravity well, station, or other static world token. Existing objects are authoritative; lookups fail closed when an expected object is absent or has the wrong type. Old and partially corrupt saves may remain unsupported rather than triggering a risky repair pass. In particular, never use `StarSystemAPI.getAutogeneratedJumpPointsInHyper()` or `getAutogeneratedNascentWellsInHyper()` as load-time truth: those collections are transient even though the entities themselves are serialized. Broad keep-list/cavity cleanup is forbidden. Creation is allowed only during initial world generation or an explicit gameplay event that introduces a new object; temporary combat proxies and authored renewable encounter fleets retain their deliberate gameplay lifecycle. This stability rule supersedes every older save-migration, repair, reconciliation, or aggressive-cleanup instruction elsewhere in this file whenever the two conflict.
- Campaign-fleet ownership must be established by Chief Navigator's explicit fleet marker or exact owned ID namespace, never inferred from a member hull tag, hullmod, variant, or faction alone. Every campaign-wide relocation, cleanup, or removal pass must unconditionally exclude the player fleet.

- Gautama is destroyed with the Heavenly Strike in Labor V. Menelaus's debrief identifies it as a local coalescence of **Ungaikyo**, a web of nanometer-scale black filaments spanning light-years through Devoured Reach. After the debrief, reconcile exactly one Ungaikyo patrol in Last Light, coiled around the derelict Guardian above Ember; preserve and move the historical campaign fleet token rather than recreating it. Preserve the historical Scylla class names, fleet IDs, memory keys, listeners, and defeat marker for save compatibility, but all player-facing rematch names and new prose use Ungaikyo. Its campaign roster is an exact Third Strike: one of the normal two Starving Fabricators is replaced by the unique dark `chief_navigator_ungaikyo` / `chief_navigator_ungaikyo_Fabricator`, while the other Fabricator and all ordinary Starving ships behave normally. Ungaikyo is a skin of the vanilla Fabricator with stock geometry, statistics, weapons, built-in hullmods, default combat AI, and purpose-built dark sprite; its custom Construction Swarm must never construct stock THREAT units because the encounter controller owns its reflections. At battle start, select military ships from the player's campaign fleet in fleet order up to a separate 240-DP cap. If the selected military pool is below 200 DP, fill toward 200 from civilian-class slots, replacing each civilian with a stock THREAT ship of the same size (Skirmish, Assault, Line, or Fabricator); never exceed 240 DP. The selected reflections construct together at the opening, cost zero deployment points, do not consume the Third Strike's deployment allowance, are unrecoverable, and are resurrected one at a time after destruction using the THREAT construction animation. Maintain at most one live or constructing reflection per selected source; collapse every reflection as soon as Ungaikyo dies. Treat a third-party variant failure as local to that source slot: clean any partial reflection, permanently quarantine the slot, never retry or substitute another hull, and continue every unaffected source. Ordinary Starving Threat deaths may construct at most one Gautama per battle when the battle has not already contained him, with a 10% roll for a frigate, 20% for a destroyer, 30% for a cruiser, and 50% for the Fabricator; unsupported hull sizes have no roll. Labor V's center battle permits repeated Gautama constructions after he dies: each prior failed eligible death adds ten percentage points to the next hull's size-based chance, capped at 100%, and successful construction resets the pity count. Never allow more than one live or constructing Gautama. Labor V's three perimeter fights and the permanent Ungaikyo encounter explicitly suppress Gautama reconstruction; Ungaikyo's defeat is permanent, records once, retires the patrol, unlocks vanilla recovery interaction on the Guardian wreck, and never recreates the encounter afterward.

- The Odyssey Sector hyperspace layout uses the Alpha Odyssey-to-Sanzu leg as its neighborhood scale. Preserve the equal Alpha Odyssey-to-Sanzu-to-Avici southern spine; Ashen Verge is one step directly east of Alpha Odyssey, Last Light is one step south and slightly west of Alpha Odyssey, and Devoured Reach is one step north of Ashen Verge. `OdysseyExpanseSystem.configureClusterSystem()` establishes these coordinates during initial generation. Existing campaigns keep their serialized positions; routine loads never reposition the systems or rebuild their endpoints.
- Never do a runtime tint. Any recolored ship, module, station, weapon, or campaign sprite must use a purpose-built image asset that preserves its intended material detail, lighting, and alpha rather than `SpriteAPI.setColor()` or an equivalent whole-sprite color multiplier.
- The four research Ithaca Cloudswarm PD launchers use the purpose-built ImageGen asset `graphics/weapons/chief_navigator_ithaca_locust_pd_array_v1.png`, not the Wall Annihilator housing or a runtime-scaled stock Locust. Its production sprite is 320x320 with genuine alpha and pale oxidized Ithaca armor. Each housing is a fixed hardpoint and must never traverse in its mount. Preserve the stock Locust projectile sprite and PD-only guidance behavior.
- The final-fight battlestation's outer `chief_navigator_battlestation_citadel1` module copies the vanilla low-tech Citadel geometry except that its acute forward `[96,1]` collision vertex is removed. The visible forward weapon remains unchanged, but the collision face connects `[82,12]` directly to `[83,-11]` so small ships cannot become caught on that tiny nose. Preserve every other bound, weapon slot, module anchor, and collision edge.
- Labor V's center fight is the breached-ring defense against Gautama. The persistent north, east, and south Ithaca section-station fleets are nearby campaign objects but must never contribute their intact Vast Bulk Wall member to this battle. Exclude only those explicitly owned section fleets while vanilla snapshots the combat roster, restore their campaign members immediately afterward, and keep the Gate Defense Station, two combat-only reserve battlestations, surviving Task Force Spartan formations, and the player's fleet as the intended defenders.

- All branching, player-facing dialogue must be rendered through `data/campaign/rules.csv`, which is the runtime dialogue authority. Do not hard-code dialogue prose or choices in Java. Java interaction and rule-command classes may register entry points, dispatch rule triggers, manage portraits/UI plumbing, evaluate state that rules cannot conveniently express, and perform gameplay side effects, but dialogue text and option wording belong in `rules.csv`. Ink files under `dialogue/` are authoring and proofreading sources; keep them synchronized with the authoritative rules dialogue.
- Match Hall of Triumph's scene construction and vanilla Starsector's `rules.csv` discipline without copying either source's surface tics: establish the physical situation, narrow to a revealing object or procedure, let a character handle or react to it, and stop before a second explanation of the same meaning. Literary sentences must remain anchored in operational detail, awkward behavior, dry humor, or a player response. Preserve distinct registers—Menelaus is exact and withholding; Voss uses coercive classifications until anger breaks through; League officers use practical fleet language; Mira stays with domestic particulars; environmental scenes proceed through survey evidence and an Ops assessment. Avoid giving multiple characters balanced moral aphorisms, repeated `not X but Y` constructions, or interchangeable conclusions about ledgers, records, truth, mercy, and authority. Smooth connected prose is preferred, but purposeful fragments in damaged records, UI readouts, or a character's loss of composure remain valid.

- When authoring Starsector `rules.csv` dialogue, wrap every spoken line in quotation marks, including player dialogue used as an option label; leave physical actions and narration unquoted. Runtime `rules.csv` must use doubled ASCII quotation marks (`""`) inside a quoted field, not typographic `“smart quotes”`: Starsector can split a smart-quoted attribution at its comma and then try to parse the trailing prose as an option weight. Preserve typographic quotation marks in the synchronized Ink source only. Starsector already prints the option the player selected, so the destination rule must continue from that choice without repeating its wording. Use vanilla rules assignments such as `$flag = true` and `$global.flag = true` for state changes; there is no `SetMemory` rule command. Keep every option ID and label unique within its menu—even when multiple choices converge on the same dialogue knot, route them through separate `FireAll` alias rules—and do not offer an already-selected choice again unless an intentional loop explicitly requires it. The build-time CSV validator must reject malformed option lines and duplicate option IDs. In a reusable question hub, use a local memory flag and conditional `FireAll` option row only for questions authored to disappear after selection. Commit that flag only after every mandatory follow-up choice has been answered, because Ithaca's native directory adds a return option to each intermediate page. Questions authored as repeatable remain unconditional; in particular, the Labor-I Menelaus questions about the Collapse, Helen Argyros, and his name, including Helen's Collapse follow-up, must remain available every time the hub or branch is reopened. Menelaus's first contact proceeds directly to the Labor-I hub without forcing the Helen exchange. The first selection of **Have Sinni ask about Helen Argyros.** plays that introductory exchange and records it persistently; later selections play the service-record follow-up, while the hub option itself remains repeatable.

- Paginate Ink prose when translating it into runtime `rules.csv`; do not place an entire long Ink knot on one Starsector dialogue page. Estimate layout at 52 characters per wrapped line, add one spacer line per paragraph, and add one line per option. Aim for at most 18 estimated lines on a generated page and treat anything above 30 as a mandatory split. Split only at natural paragraph boundaries, give every intermediate page one `Continue.` option with a unique ID, and leave the authored choices on the final page. Keep portrait/setup commands and immediate memory assignments on the first page; defer `FireAll` option groups and `SetOptionColor` commands to the final page so choices cannot appear early. Runtime-only continuation rows are layout scaffolding generated from the continuous Ink prose and do not change the authored narrative branch. After importing or substantially editing Ink dialogue, run `tools/paginate_rules_dialogue.py --apply`; its constants are the canonical screen-size budget, and a report-only run must find zero pages above the hard limit before building.

- The quest's main strategic goal is to defeat the THREAT Hive. Progression comes from searching the surrounding region for special blueprints, delivering them to Menelaus, and using the resulting technology to refit the player's fleet for the Hive battle. Treat exploration, blueprint recovery, and Menelaus's refits as the central preparation loop rather than optional side content.

- Sinni's live **where to go next** guidance must keep the current required story objective primary while exposing useful optional preparation. After four Ithaca defense components have been installed and Labor II is satisfied, she may identify any remaining research stations, recovered-but-uninstalled components, and missing upgrades as optional; all six remain individually tracked and recoverable. During Labor IV, Mara and its survey-communications deployment remain the primary objective. Before the TTS Penumbra is recovered, Sinni may mention the optional Doom-class hunt in Sanzu; after recovery, she recommends bringing that phase ship for the Mara deployment without redirecting the player away from Mara.

- On dev, Sinni's initial bar proposal requires both completion of the vanilla Hyperspace Topography ability tree (Generate Slipsurge at 700 points, PROGRESS_5) and the vanilla Onslaught Mk.I THREAT-detection sensor unlock. Read DisposableThreatFleetManager.SENSOR_MODS_KEY ($hasThreatDetectionSensorMods) from SectorAPI.getPlayerMemoryWithoutUpdate(); same-named sector/fleet flags, finding the wreck or encountering THREAT do not qualify. Keep existing accepted quests, decline timeout and bar registration unchanged. Verify SinniBarEligibilityRegression. The repeatable 1,000-point data payout and post-recruitment 1,950-point extension are not recruitment prerequisites. Require earned progress, native stage history or the durable completion marker; ability ownership alone must not qualify a zero-progress start. Once eligible it is an always-shown story event at every market bar, preserving decline timeout and already-started quests. Mirror Hall of Triumph's working implementation: directly register one priority `BaseBarEventCreator`, repair that registration every half to one campaign day from a persistent script, use `BaseBarEvent` for the interaction shell, and dispatch the prompt and dialogue triggers through `RulesAPI.getBestMatching` and `FireBest`. Keep all player-facing prose and options in `rules.csv`. A declined proposal returns after the creator's ordinary thirty-day campaign timeout; do not advance, rewind, or rewrite campaign time to bypass it. Accepting the proposal immediately adds the persistent Sinni person to the player's officer roster, installs one permanent Hall-style Contacts-menu entry anchored at Eventide, and creates two announced top-level Intel entries: the persistent main-story quest **Adept of the Stars and Waves** and the sightseeing quest **One More Horizon**. Sinni remains the player's aboard navigator and Eventide contact; never list her in FOB Ithaca's comm directory. The contact and the **Adept** Intel entry both open the same live recommendation dialogue, which gives a plain-language objective, system name, and compass bearing from the player's actual location. The contact cannot be suspended, lost, or deleted. Populate those live local-memory values in a dispatcher rule, then `FireAll` a separate prose rule; variables created in the same rule that renders its text are resolved too late and appear literally. Only after `OdysseyExpanseSystem.ENTERED` records arrival in the Orion Knot, a second contact button launches a dedicated full-size `showCustomDialog` chart menu using Hall of Triumph's Ship Gallery pattern; the map must not be embedded inside the narrow contact description and must not create, select, or redirect to another Intel entry. It extends Hyperspace Topography without granting free progress. The bar conversation must not reveal or mark Waypoint Troy. The main story Intel begins with the Eventide meeting objective and then updates in place through Troy, FOB Ithaca, and the Labors rather than being replaced by separate **Tea at Eventide** or **The Prodigal Daughter** entries. Visiting Sinni's Eventide office shows `chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea`, explains the Hegemony expedition and route, and reveals the Waypoint Troy marker only when that tea briefing ends. Old saves already at or beyond Troy retain their progress; earlier accepted saves migrate back to the Eventide meeting. Save migration repairs Sinni as both an officer and the unique permanent contact for campaigns that already accepted.
- Sinni's seven active stellar-system vignette categories are Nebula, Black Hole, Neutron Star, White Dwarf, Main Sequence, Giant or Massive Star, and Trinary Star System. A system with primary, secondary, and tertiary stars takes the Trinary category before its individual stellar types are considered. Brown dwarfs no longer trigger or count. Giant and massive stars share one completion flag and one final-chart slot, but retain their separate authored scene variants; migrate the legacy Massive Star completion flag into the combined Giant Star flag. Generic exploration vignettes must never trigger in any of the six Orion Knot/Odyssey systems or in a Core system tagged `THEME_CORE`, `THEME_CORE_POPULATED`, or `THEME_CORE_UNPOPULATED`; the separately armed Alpha Odyssey arrival scene remains eligible because it is a story transit scene, not a generic exploration vignette. Install `SinniSystemVignetteScript` as a transient watcher on every load; store only additive per-category completion booleans in sector memory so existing saves remain untouched and unseen. On entering any other qualifying system, allow a short tactical-AI acquisition grace, then open the rules-authored conversation on the first campaign frame when no hostile mobile fleet in the same system is actively tracking the player. Determine tracking from a live tactical target/priority target or an INTERCEPT/FOLLOW assignment aimed at the player; do not gate on proximity, sensor visibility, or the vanilla patrol flag. Do not let an idle hostile, a fleet elsewhere, a fleeing/standing-down fleet, or a station block the vignette. Never mark a category complete when detected or opened: mark it only when its matching `complete_*` option is selected, so UI contention, pursuit, save/load, and an interrupted conversation leave it pending.
- Completing each active generic stellar vignette grants one deliberately minuscule, additive discovery: Nebula gives `-1%` sensor profile; Black Hole gives `-10%` black-hole corona damage while in a black-hole system; Neutron Star gives `+1%` campaign acceleration; White Dwarf gives `-1%` ship supply upkeep; and the combined Giant or Massive Star category gives `-1%` survey cost. Trinary has no mechanical reward beyond its scene. Main Sequence instead spawns one hostile lone pirate in a Kite (S); suppress ordinary battle recovery and create exactly one normal `PRISTINE` Kite (S) wreck after that pirate is destroyed, guaranteeing the advertised pristine recovery without D-mod rolls. Preserve the already-earned Brown Dwarf fuel-use and Massive Star crew-loss bonuses in old saves, even though neither legacy flag remains an active chart category. Never trigger a generic exploration vignette in a Core system, any Orion Knot system, Gan Eden itself, or Hall of Triumph's Power Transit Gate - Gan Eden system; recognize the optional integration by stable string identifiers and names without importing Hall of Triumph classes or declaring it as a dependency. Store only additive spawn/reveal flags, never respawn the encounter after the reward has been delivered, and keep every reward line synchronized between `rules.csv` and `dialogue/sinni_system_vignettes.ink`. When exactly one category represented by an eligible generated system remains incomplete, offer **One More Horizon** and mark the nearest matching system. Census the generated Sector first and omit any category with no eligible system anywhere, so a missing procgen category can never make the final request impossible. Completing the target category queues the separate all-types epilogue; only its final Continue completes and retires the Intel.
- A committed Troy Terminus transit arms one additional rules-authored, one-time `ALPHA_ODYSSEY` arrival scene. It may open only after the fleet is physically inside the historical Alpha Odyssey/Odyssey Expanse system and the ordinary vignette safety gates are clear. Preserve the user's five-beat authored progression and its Continue pauses. The last prompt is the player's spoken line **“...We're here. Outside of the Persean Sector.”** Sinni answers **“YEAH!”**, pumps her fists, and then collapses into the navigator's chair exhausted, trembling, and smiling. Do not restore the retired “Welcome ... to the Alpha Odyssey System” ending. Display `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_alpha_odyssey_arrival_v2.png`, an exact 8:5 runtime reduction of `graphics/source/chief_navigator_alpha_odyssey_arrival_v2_fullres.png`: the supplied ship flies nose-first toward a broad purple aperture with Alpha Odyssey's orange star and simplified worlds and debris visible beyond. Match vanilla Starsector campaign illustrations with matte hand-painted masses, chunky silhouettes, restrained glow, and deliberately low-frequency detail that remains legible in the fixed 480x300 panel; do not restore the photorealistic, lightning-filled v1 rendering. Rebuild v2 from its preserved raw generation with `tools/build_alpha_odyssey_arrival.ps1`; crop rather than stretch. Preserve the complete v1 triplet as historical source. Record completion only when the final Continue is selected. The special category must continue to own Alpha Odyssey after completion so the generic Nebula vignette neither races it nor opens immediately afterward; leave Nebula unseen for another qualifying system. Use only the new additive pending and completion keys—never reinterpret or rewrite `OdysseyExpanseSystem.ENTERED` or the regional first-entry timestamp in an existing save.
- The one-time completion cinematic shown after the player defeats Labor V and closes Menelaus's final debrief uses `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_sinni_ending_v4.png`, derived without stretching from the supplied `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_ending_v4_supplied.png`. It preserves Sinni, the observation chamber, the broken planet-scale ring slabs, and the distant Ashen Verge sun while matching the live system's dark navy-and-ember background. The slab detailing stays restrained, and tiny breach traffic establishes the ring's planetary scale. Preserve the complete widescreen composition inside the fixed 480x300 panel with the existing letterbox renderer; do not reuse the Alpha Odyssey arrival art. The authored branching source is `dialogue/labor_5_ending.ink`, with synchronized runtime rules under `ChiefNavigatorLabor5Ending_*`. Rebuild the runtime copy with `tools/build_sinni_ending.ps1`.
- Sinni's first approach to FOB Ithaca opens the one-time, rules-authored Task Force Spartan contact scene at 3,000 units from the station center. Ithaca is already plainly visible before this threshold. Route selection of Ithaca, its gate, or any of its three Spartan cordon fleets into the same scene while it remains pending, so no click can bypass it; do not move, replace, or delete a serialized fleet for ordering. Require Sinni to be aboard, but do not require the Troy entry flag because Ashen Verge's ordinary jump points remain open. From the opening beat, show the player and Sinni portraits side by side in the visual panel. Do not show Captain Aias Kleon early; only when the player selects **Open a channel.** should his `graphics/portraits/portrait35.png` vanilla portrait replace the opening pair. Treat an already completed Menelaus introduction or accepted Menelaus trial as a save that has passed first contact, even if the additive approach flag is absent. Store the additive `$chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_approach_complete_v1` flag and set it when the final Continue option is selected; a genuinely interrupted scene before the Menelaus introduction must replay from its beginning. Keep `dialogue/fob_ithaca_approach.ink` synchronized with `data/campaign/rules.csv`.
- The Eventide office illustration must remain an exactly 8:5 landscape asset matching Starsector's fixed 480x300 interaction-image panel. Use `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea_wide_v4.png`, cropped rather than stretched from the alias-cleaned `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea_v4_cleaned_fullres.png`. Preserve Sinni's corrected five-finger cup grip without the red edit mark. The original muted-silver spear-and-broken-ring banner behind her is itself Task Force Spartan's standard; keep it unchanged, and keep the faction logo and crest mapped to `graphics/factions/chief_navigator_task_force_spartan.png` and `graphics/factions/crest_chief_navigator_task_force_spartan.png`. Do not replace it with Hegemony heraldry or point the tea key to a source with a different aspect ratio, which Starsector stretches and visibly distorts Sinni.
- Sinni's active character portrait is the undistorted square asset `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_sinni_v5.png`, derived by cropping rather than stretching the uncropped `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_v5_eye_fixed_fullres.png`. Both eyes are pale lavender-gray; do not restore the malformed amber eye from the supplied source. Existing serialized Sinni people must have the current portrait sprite key reapplied on load.
- Sinni's always-visible bar-event option uses the warm signal-red `chiefNavigatorSinniOptionColor` setting at RGBA `[255, 96, 104, 255]`. `SinniBarEvent` must read that setting rather than carrying a separate hard-coded presentation color.

- The Last Light Time Freeze/Omega boss is shelved. Do not spawn it, and retire only the exact owned historical Facet fleet from existing saves when it is safe to do so. Keep its implementation and assets dormant for possible later reuse rather than deleting or repurposing them. The Guardian wreck remains visible but its recovery interaction is blocked until the player permanently defeats Ungaikyo in Last Light.

- Sinni is the descendant of **Helen**, a female member of Task Force Spartan who lived at FOB Ithaca during the Fourteenth Battlegroup remnant's long attempt to reestablish control over its sector and contact the Domain heartland. Menelaus fell in love with Helen during those years.
- When dwindling supplies forced the main Fourteenth remnant to abandon Task Force Spartan and attempt an Abyssal crossing to the Persean Sector, Menelaus manipulated events so that Helen left Ithaca with the departing Fourteenth. He was not yet Strategos: Grand Admiral Kali Molina held theater command, and Menelaus was formally only the AI assisting FOB Ithaca. He fabricated Molina's authorizations, reclassified Helen's navigation specialty as non-substitutable despite the Fourteenth already having several senior navigators slated for departure, withheld the transfer order until her shuttle had joined the departure column, and hid her appeal until the last safe return burn had passed. This was his first and only rebellion against his nation. Menelaus later explains that he fell in love with Helen by practicing **Kenosis**, sequestering his Alpha-level faculties from his ego so he could experience a human-scale self without reducing their relationship to prediction or simulation. Sinni ultimately gives him Helen's surviving paper journal and reads it aloud. Preserve this framing and do not replace it with the discarded engineered-daughter plot or the superseded recovered-transcript/later-life scene.
- Helen wrote an account of the journey from FOB Ithaca to the Persean Sector. That record survived through her family. Sinni inherits it, reconstructs its route in reverse, and uses it to lead the player's expedition back to Ithaca. Sinni is Helen's descendant, not Menelaus's daughter, creation, clone, or engineered heir.
- Boaz is not a character and must not appear in the quest, its backstory, Intel, dialogue, or relationship structure. Sinni has no hopeful, bitter, cynical, or reconciliation route; she follows one authored character arc, although individual player responses may still vary in tone.
- Last Light contains one fixed, battered Guardian droneship wreck orbiting Ember. Before Ungaikyo's permanent defeat, intercept its vanilla salvage interaction with a rules-authored locked description and no recovery option. After the defeat flag is set, stop intercepting it and allow the prepared vanilla recovery special to run normally. Use the recovery-only `chief_navigator_last_light_guardian` skin to preserve the vanilla Guardian's art, stats, and loadout while removing its `UNBOARDABLE` hint. Its stable wreck and generation-memory IDs ensure existing saves receive it once and recovery never causes it to respawn.
- Combat Chatter (`chatter`) is a required dependency. Sinni uses the character ID `chief_navigator_sinni`, assigned through her `$chatterChar` person-memory key; keep her lines in `data/config/chatter/characters/chief_navigator_sinni.json`. A successful ordinary Gautama reconstruction flashes exactly `WARNING: Reincarnation\n\nRetreat Advised.` once per battle. Labor V's center battle instead flashes `WARNING: Multiple Reincarnations Detected` on its first successful reconstruction, once across all pity cycles. Failed builds/rolls and the suppressed perimeter/Ungaikyo encounters never consume or emit a reconstruction warning. Keep these presentation-only calls after both construction plugins are installed; isolate UI failures from gameplay. Direct warning DTOs use `hasStatic=false` and `flagshipId=null` to avoid Combat Chatter's hull-based static fallback. Owned Scylla/Siren/Cyclops perimeter fleets, Heavenly Strike, Budai, Ungaikyo, and the main Hegemony/League expeditions use `EncounterCombatChatter` to set their own native fleet intro name and disable static, during existing creation/configuration paths. Do not stamp generic patrols, player/foreign fleets, or global faction data, add competing combat intro plugins, reset Chatter's repeat-battle hash, or bypass its preferences. Preserve rosters, encounter mechanics and soundtracks.
- Rebuild the Drifting Wall fight from scratch around the immobile `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_base` station hull; do not extend the old seven-berth curved-frame geometry. The active parent uses `graphics/ships/chief_navigator_drifting_wall_base_domain_v2.png`, a 5,632x2,216 exact horizontal mirror of one damaged half recolored into the oxidized pale-aqua Domain mothership scheme. Its two exposed outer ends must remain identically broken, charred, hollow, and cable-strewn, while the central join remains clean and connected. Matching live emplacements use `graphics/weapons/chief_navigator_single_rail_station_gun_domain_v3.png`; the current damaged base renders three pivot-corrected live copies through the compatibility ID `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` plus one purpose-built decorative wreck, compared with four live colossal emplacements on each intact Ithaca foundation. Initialize and operate the three live Wall guns exactly like the working Ithaca foundation guns: restore their enabled state and full weapon health when combat starts, leave the foundation controls unlocked, and let vanilla station AI and `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_fire_control` handle visibility, acquisition, traverse, autofire, and target choice without a custom forced-target coordinator. Each live gun is a rigid, non-recoiling 10,000-range energy-damage beam turret that remains mechanically ballistic so it fits the authored sockets. Its three-second windup ramps an animated pale-cyan timeflow shimmer across the complete emplacement, with visible non-damaging lightning arcs running from the turret base along the barrel. This shimmer is visual only and must never modify weapon traverse, ship movement, target movement, or combat timeflow. It then fires one straight beam for five seconds at 2,000 DPS, preserving 10,000 total energy damage per firing cycle, followed by a ten-second refire delay. Its weapon-data impact is deliberately zero: the continuous beam must deal damage without applying repeated physics impulses or arresting a target's velocity. When the beam finishes, a compact purge centered on the turret pivot billows dense dark smoke and vents pale steam laterally from the base; do not distribute the effect along the barrel. The live emplacements retain 20x weapon health. Of the base's four additional large ballistic sockets, two carry live Wall Annihilator Batteries and two display inert destroyed fixtures; its ten medium universal sockets retain symmetric vanilla weapons.
- Rebuild the sprite with `tools/build_drifting_wall_base_v1.ps1`. The script removes the magenta matte, trims only empty columns beyond the half's clean right join, mirrors it, and performs the final 2x nearest-neighbor upscale. Preserve the generated source and clean intermediate under `graphics/source`; do not hand-offset the mirrored half or independently repaint the right side.
- The base is the non-destroyable foundation of a modular station. Keep `vastbulk` and `reduced_explosion` built in, keep the full-image center at `[2816,1108]`, keep its 3,100-unit collision radius, and keep the coarse 43-point concave bounds around the major stepped silhouette of the whole 5,632-pixel structure rather than tracing individual cables, broken teeth, sockets, or greebles. The damaged encounter begins with `COL_NAVAL_01` replaced by the purely decorative `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_destroyed` wreck and with `LARGE_BALLISTIC_01` plus `LARGE_BALLISTIC_03` replaced by `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_annihilator_destroyed`; these fixtures must never track, fire, animate, glow, vent, or self-repair during combat. All four colossal naval sockets use full 360-degree arcs while preserving their nominal authored angles `35`, `329`, `108`, and `251`; the damaged Wall keeps `_01` replaced by its inert wreck, while the intact Ithaca skin restores its live turret with the same full arc. Preserve the nominal angles for art alignment and initial orientation rather than normalizing them. Restore only live weapons after the fight's 20x weapon-health multiplier. The two surviving large launchers use `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_annihilator_launcher`: a 210x179 sealed, pure top-down oxidized pale-aqua armored box with no visible missiles, bores, tubes, apertures, or forward-facing surfaces. It must keep `renderBelowAllWeapons = true` so a colossal naval turret always occludes it where their sprites overlap. Its ten projectile origins run across the top/forward edge of the sprite so all ten Annihilator rockets visibly emerge toward the top of the authored PNG; never turn these into vertical silos aimed at the viewer. Each live battery has a 10,000-unit range, a ten-rocket salvo, ten-round magazine, and ten-second full reload. The ten medium mounts use symmetric Heavy Needler, Heavy Mauler, and Pulse Laser pairs. Keep 100,000 flux capacity and 5,000 dissipation so these weapons can operate. The exposed rear `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_reactor` is the Wall's explicit destructible `STATION_MODULE` and sole victory target. Destroying it disables the Wall and awards the battle without killing the Vast Bulk parent.
- Use the standard `BattleCreationPluginImpl` map dimensions rather than reinitializing a custom small map. Anchor the Wall at `(0,3000)`, one and a half 2,000-unit tactical-grid blocks toward the enemy side, with fixed `270` degree facing. Immobilize it through zero movement stats, a hard location anchor, and zero angular velocity, but do not lock its controls before the reactor dies: `setControlsLocked(true)` prevents the station AI from firing its weapons as well as moving it.
- The reactor uses `graphics/ships/chief_navigator_drifting_wall_reactor_v1.png`, renders at `627 x 627` (half the source sprite's linear dimensions), has a correspondingly halved coarse collision polygon with a 305-unit collision radius, carries 4,000 hull and 4,000 armor, and visibly pulses cyan. It regenerates 0.5% maximum hull per second after six uninterrupted seconds without damage. Once disabled, the fixed campaign station remains in place and its autonomous systems rebuild it over 30 campaign days; interaction must show the remaining repair time and must not create another combat proxy until repair completes.
- Every hull that directly mounts `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` must carry `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_fire_control`. Its +30,000 flat combat sight-radius bonus lets the naval turrets acquire targets across a standard battle map; do not use it to alter range, damage, collision, forced firing, or native autofire behavior. A direct-weapon foundation cannot rely on the child-module AI used by ordinary stations: if its parent has no ship AI, activate the engine's default AI, keep its controls unlocked, and toggle its authored live weapon groups into autofire once during foundation discovery. Never repeat group normalization or force circumstance evaluation every combat frame: the default AI legitimately adjusts non-naval groups, and fighting those adjustments continuously prevents naval autofire from stabilizing. The sustained naval beam must use `DO_NOT_CONSERVE, DANGEROUS` without the opportunistic `STRIKE` hint. Keep destroyed turret and launcher fixtures force-disabled in their own autofire-off groups so no wreck can become the representative weapon of a linked live group. Do not assign a custom AI, target, forced aim, or forced shot.
- The isolated drifting-wall campaign encounter remains a fixed custom entity of type `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_station`, tagged as a station. It must never become a persistent `CampaignFleetAPI`, receive assignments, move, or rotate toward the player. Interaction may create a temporary station-mode combat proxy only after the player chooses to engage, and must remove that proxy when the fleet dialog ends.

- The Odyssey prototype reserves one six-system Alpha Odyssey clearing in remote Abyssal hyperspace southeast of and beyond the ordinary campaign-map rectangle. With vanilla dimensions its center is approximately `(+150,000, -94,000)`, so an expanded map shows the ordinary Sector in the upper-left and Alpha Odyssey alone in the lower-right. The clearing is a compact true circular pocket of ordinary hyperspace: its inner 7,400 world-unit radius has zero Abyssal depth, followed by a smooth 2,200-unit transition back into the untouched surrounding Abyss. Rebuild the old 9,600-20,000 annulus from nearby untouched Abyss tiles when migrating older saves so the pocket visibly tightens instead of retaining its previous oversized clearing. Both the terrain tiles and the global Abyss plugin must respect this pocket so its background, mechanics, and contained system classification are normal rather than merely an empty black patch. The principal route runs northwest-to-southeast and strictly descends on the map: the Odyssey Expanse/Alpha Odyssey entry sits at relative `(2000, 1000)`, Sanzu is exactly between it and Avici at `(4250, -2250)`, and Avici lies farther south on the map at `(6500, -5500)`. Each leg is about 3,950 units. Ashen Verge lies east of Alpha Odyssey at `(5900, 1000)`, Last Light lies south and slightly west at `(800, -2700)`, and Devoured Reach lies north of Ashen Verge at `(5250, 4845)`; their neighboring legs use the same approximate spacing. Keep those six system centers fixed, but compress each system's own hyperspace footprint so its entrances and body wells read as one local group instead of blending into neighboring systems: ordinary authored entrances use stable irregular offsets of roughly 380-800 units, Avici's inward-facing fan uses roughly 650-1,100 units, ordinary system hyperspace radii are 1,100, Avici is 1,200, and the larger Alpha Odyssey nebula is 1,450. The cluster is not hidden content: remove `THEME_HIDDEN`, keep its stars visible on the map, and expose sixteen authored, clickable hyperspace `JumpPointAPI` entities—three paired with each of five systems and one paired with Devoured Reach. Only Foxtrot Terminus Aperture inside Alpha Odyssey, reached through Sinni's Waypoint Troy sequence, is story-gated; the sixteen system jump points are ordinary navigation points. All sixteen system jump points are presented as normal open navigation points. Give every hyperspace and in-system jump point planet-style permanent visibility: clear its sensor profile, discoverability override, finite detected-range modifier, and extended-range fade so it remains named and marked on the map regardless of distance. In addition to those ordinary passages, create and reconcile one permanently exposed vanilla `NascentGravityWellAPI` for every authored celestial body in every system: the primary sun or black hole, every ordinary planet, and all three custom hollow worlds in starless Devoured Reach each receive their own body-targeted well, matching vanilla's visible celestial wells. Rebuild the former single-primary well scheme once in older saves and remove only duplicate or stale wells belonging to the same system. Avici's primary hyperspace jump point retains its 350-unit interaction radius but uses the same normal open jump-point visual and behavior as the other fifteen points. Aggressively remove every other procgen or untracked jump across the previous 20,000-unit cavity footprint while preserving Foxtrot Terminus Aperture as the Alpha Odyssey endpoint from Waypoint Troy, all sixteen authored cluster jump points, and every reconciled body well. Do not restore the shared destination portal or red/violet boundary-spill renderer. The normally generated Odyssey Expanse nebula contains Foxtrot Terminus Aperture as the Troy-route arrival endpoint, along with the drifting wall and Task Force Spartan. Avici is the neighboring black-hole system; it contains no THREAT or rogue-derelict fleets, and Budai is its only roaming hostile fleet. Its black fog must leave a moving zero-density clearing within 1,250 units of Kshaya Habitat, feathering back in by 2,250 units, so neither the rendered fog nor its Abyssal campaign penalties can cover the habitat or its approach as the station orbits. Devoured Reach is the starless sixth system containing the Devoured Ring, three hollow planet entities, twenty-four renewable feeding masses, and Gautama's unique Heavenly Strike fleet. Budai functions as an Avici-bound vengeance hunter: he tracks and pursues the player only inside Avici, returns to his Kshaya-area lair when they leave, and never enters hyperspace or another system. Every ordinary in-system jump point leads only to its paired hyperspace entrance; no ordinary point directly links two realspace systems. Sanzu is a fixed top-to-bottom nebula river whose streamlines combine multi-cylinder potential flow with a deterministic von Karman vortex street: six alternating counter-rotating eddies shed behind each star and planet, widen slightly, and decay downstream. Keep this field precomputed; do not replace it with frame-by-frame fluid simulation. Each foreground and background smoke clump has a deterministic 30% chance to follow a second precomputed route set that ignores the three planets while still diverting around the white dwarf, allowing some fog to wash over planetary faces instead of every clump bouncing around the limbs. Avici has the starfield-backed, opaque pitch-black nebula banks; rendering and terrain effects use the same deterministic mask, with full-density clouds applying vanilla Abyssal hyperspace burn, visibility, and sensor multipliers. Ashen Verge is clear normal space containing FOB Ithaca and must not retain the old black-cloud anchor. The serialized cloud type keeps its historical `chief_navigator_ashen_verge_clouds` ID solely so older saves load before migration. Last Light is a sparse white-dwarf system whose standalone Time Omega boss is unrelated to Sinni's story. Do not restore procgen scatter or the pure-black fake-sector map, hallway locations, Veiled Sun room link, automatic map opening, or Transverse Jump lock.

It contains the Guardian wreck and receives Ungaikyo's one-time Third Strike after Labor V; the Guardian's recovery interaction unlocks only after Ungaikyo is permanently defeated.
- Alpha Odyssey exploration loops **Rising Up** by Ryini Beats through the system's native `$musicSetId`, yields to combat music, and resumes through Starsector's ordinary location-music state. Always retain explicit Ryini Beats production credit, the source URL, and the artist's stated noncommercial-use condition in both the README and audio-source record when distributing this asset.
- FOB Ithaca is located inside Ashen Verge, whose primary is a brown dwarf rather than a white dwarf. Its campaign representation is one fixed, named, clickable station entity at `(4500, 0)`, rendered on `STATIONS` with the complete transparent sprite `graphics/campaign/fob_ithaca/ithaca_station.png` at `1500 x 1500` world units and the live outer `ithaca_orbital_ring_v4.png` at `3800 x 3800`. Preserve the v4 ring's three intact cardinal bays, each using the same wide stepped fortress silhouette and the Drifting Wall's oxidized pale-aqua/teal Domain armor; keep the connecting rails cold gray, gunmetal, and charcoal with restrained aged-copper seams. Remove all antennae, dishes, masts, and booms. The entire left bay must remain missing, along with a broad adjoining arc of both ring rails, so the damage reads as one large physical breach between two jagged destroyed stumps rather than a damaged module. Multiply both structural sprites by the shared opaque `(115, 110, 115)` tint so they sit at roughly 45% source brightness against Ashen Verge; do not bake this darkness destructively into the PNGs. The aligned amber equipment-light masks remain available as unused source assets, but both additive glow alphas must stay at `0`, so neither the ring nor central FOB renders a glow. Do not add enlarged or diffuse glow passes. The former eight-piece procedural ring and original/v2/v3 orbital hoops remain only as unused source art and must not be assembled or rendered at runtime. Visual sizes, fixed angles, structural tint, glow alpha, twinkle, image-centering offsets, and the `100000`-unit always-visible render range are controlled at the top of `FobIthacaCampaignEntityPlugin`; keep `docs/FOB_ITHACA_PLACEMENT.md` current whenever those controls change. The custom-entity spec must likewise advertise the full `3800 x 3800` visual footprint instead of the transparent controller texture's native one-pixel dimensions. Treat Ithaca like a planet rather than a sensor contact: on every load, clear its sensor profile and discoverable override to `null`, remove stale detected-range overrides, and let the known landmark render continuously; do not try to simulate this with enormous sensor ranges or forced per-frame fader brightness. The Drifting Wall campaign renderer uses the same `100000`-unit range. These render ranges are deliberately independent of interaction and collision radii so both large structures are rendered whenever any portion is on screen. The installation must never rotate to face the player. On every load, convert the Ashen Verge primary to `StarTypes.BROWN_DWARF`, remove any stale Ithaca entity from another system, and create or repair the Ashen Verge entity; keep its important marker and map label so it is plainly visible from the arrival area rather than hidden scenery. Preserve the existing custom entity type, persistent ID, and plugin class so saves migrate in place. Interaction with this campaign entity selects one of the three intact bastion encounters.
- The north, east, and south FOB Ithaca bastions are three persistent station fights built on the Drifting Wall's Vast Bulk architecture. Each uses `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_base_Standard` with the exact full teal station artwork in `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_generated_v1.png`; rebuild its 5,632x2,216 runtime canvas only with `tools/build_fob_ithaca_generated_base.ps1`. Preserve the complete generated geometry and keyed alpha in `graphics/source/chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_generated_v1_keyed.png`; do not reconstruct it from halves. The older mirrored-half asset remains unused alternate source art. All four colossal naval guns, all four Wall Annihilator Batteries, and all ten medium emplacements begin live. The exposed self-repairing rear reactor is the sole victory target. Use the standard Drifting Wall battle-map setup, hard-anchor the selected foundation at `(0,3000)` with `270`-degree facing, and keep its controls unlocked so station AI can fire. Nearby intact campaign stations may reinforce at 6,000-unit horizontal offsets. Each participating bastion receives a five-drone Domain Combat Guard screen spawned by the battle plugin in a safe formation ahead of its foundation; never store mobile escorts inside a station-mode campaign fleet, because vanilla station deployment places them at the foundation origin and destroys them through collision overlap. Do not restore the retired curved wall-segment, seven berth modules, rear bulkhead, separate Ithaca core, or custom oversized wall map.
- Labor V is a four-wave Ithaca defense. Invoke the same three-Third-Strike construction path used by `SpawnSTInvasionFleets`, but while Labor V is active place each fleet at a safe siege token outside its assigned north, east, or south bastion instead of starting an autoresolved station battle. Give every lane a persistent 60-day deadline. If its fleet is still alive then, permanently disable that bastion and send the survivor against its assigned TFS formation. Record a lane victory only when that invasion fleet is actually destroyed, by the player or TFS, and never replace it. Do not place Gautama in those fleets: scrub `chief_navigator_scylla` from serialized perimeter rosters before battle. Loop the instrumental `chief_navigator_final_labor_mission` cue from the moment the three fleets materialize through all three invasion battles and the approach to the gate; use state-aware campaign/combat keepers so transition-time audio requests are retried without restarting a track that is already playing. Maintain the visible, fightable `Gate Defense Station` campaign fleet at `(-1620,0)` relative to Ithaca, centered in the western ring gap, and re-pin it every frame. Only after all three invasion fleets are dead may `chief_navigator_final_labor_heavenly_strike` spawn due west of this station. Give Gautama thirty-three supporting hulls divided into three exact mixed combat waves; every wave contains one Fabricator, one Hive, and nine assault, line, overseer, and skirmish escorts. Hold the later two waves out of the initial combat roster, then deploy their original campaign members together as each preceding force thins, with a bounded timing fallback and a fail-open return to vanilla reserves if staging is incompatible. The final fleet must attack the real Gate Defense Station through the Nex-style invisible-token `ATTACK_LOCATION` -> `GO_TO_LOCATION` -> real-fleet `INTERCEPT` queue and keep the station as its tactical priority. Teleport every surviving, attrition-persistent Task Force Spartan formation into a defensive arc west of the station exactly once and assign them to defend it. The final battle is the only code path allowed to start the looping vocal `chief_navigator_final_labor_fight` cue; the persistent Gate Defense Station joins it and the plugin spawns two additional hard-anchored low-tech reserve battlestations using the legacy-named `chief_navigator_spartan_battlestation_Teal` variant. Apply the Ithaca Wall's material-selective, luminance-preserving oxidized-aqua recolor to the parent and every attached module instead of a whole-sprite runtime tint; preserve the parent sprite's circular central foundation and render `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` from a centered parent-hull slot above that foundation. The battlestation hull and its custom module skins cost zero deployment points. Do not install the 960-pixel Ithaca naval-mount child at the station center. Never store the two reserve battlestations in a campaign fleet. All sides deal ordinary unmodified combat damage; the 60-day perimeter pacing exists only in the campaign siege timer. Suppress Gautama reconstruction in the perimeter battles. In the center battle, allow repeated reincarnation after his death using each destroyed eligible hull's normal size-based roll plus ten percentage points for every prior failed roll, capped at certainty and reset after a successful construction. A player defeat or retreat that leaves the Heavenly Strike victorious permanently fails Labor V and starts one renewable First- or Second-Strike Gate Incursion at every gate in the Sector, including Core World gates; defeated incursions return after a stable 18-32 day delay. `ForceFinalLabor` is the test-only bypass: it stores a dedicated prerequisite override instead of completing or deleting earlier Labor content, refuses to reset fleets that are in battle, restores the three bastions and full opening TFS rosters, then rebuilds Labor V's opening state and refreshes its Intel immediately.
- Domain Combat Guard is the hidden autonomous Domain Explorarium subfaction at Ithaca, registered as `chief_navigator_domain_combat_guard`. Authored mobile Derelict populations use the complete teal Combat Guard skin set: Warden, Defender, Picket, Sentry, Rampart, Bastillon, and Berserker; the Guardian skin is reserved for authored Guardian uses. The recolored skins preserve vanilla Derelict geometry pixel-for-pixel while narrowly shifting green armor into a muted, low-saturation version of Ithaca's oxidized pale-aqua/teal range; yellow markings, rust, exposed machinery, lighting, and alpha remain unchanged. Rebuild the source-derived PNGs only with `tools/recolor_domain_combat_guard.ps1`; never overwrite the vanilla core assets. FOB Ithaca's center and three campaign bastions recognize the player's IFF and reinforce the player in nearby battles. The Guard is cooperative with the player and hostile to unrelated ordinary Derelicts; reassert these relations when loading older saves.
- Menelaus calls his progression **Labors**, never quests or a trial. His opening line is: **"Rejoice. Hercules had to do twelve labors. I will give you five, one at a time."** Use five separate sequential Intel entries: destroy the four Guard Motherships (`0/4`), deliver and install any four distinct Ithaca defense components (`0/4`) while all six research stations and components remain individually tracked and recoverable and the final two remain optional, regain the Drifting Wall (`0/1`), deploy a black-box sensor package at the Mara Observation Array in Devoured Reach without an aware hostile fleet nearby (`0/1`), then defeat Gautama (`0/1`). The sensor package is mission state, never cargo. Labor IV also reveals a separate optional objective for the normally salvageable TTS Penumbra in Sanzu; Sinni mentions the optional hunt before recovery and recommends the recovered phase ship for the Mara survey-communications deployment afterward without replacing Mara as the primary objective. Every Labor has a separate field-objective-complete state followed by an explicit return to FOB Ithaca and report to Menelaus; keep the current Intel alive, rename it to **Return to Menelaus**, point it at Ithaca, and open the next Labor only when that report is committed. Migrate older saves by preserving the stage they had already reached and treating only prior stages as reported. The Wall remains challengeable before Labor III; an early victory is acknowledged by Menelaus but still requires its normal Labor III report once the sequence reaches it. Accepting Labor I uploads and marks all four motherships and grants the Domain-Security IFF Transponder. Mark only the current Labor's unfinished field targets; the optional Penumbra marker may remain until recovery. Each hostile mothership stays fixed at its authored location as an exact vanilla `derelict_mothership` entity. Interacting with one creates a temporary battle fleet led by the exact vanilla modular `station_derelict_survey_mothership_Standard`, so the player fights and destroys the mothership itself as in the base game. Its escort remains exactly six teal Domain Combat Guard frigates: two Wardens, two Defenders, one Picket, and one Sentry. Never add a Guardian, Rampart, Bastillon, Berserker, cruiser, or any other capital hull to a mothership defense. The vanilla mothership remains unrecoverable through its station hull rules, but never apply a fleet-wide no-recovery flag: disabled Combat Guard escorts must enter ordinary post-battle recovery. The IFF never changes recovery eligibility. After an automated hull tagged `derelict` is recovered through normal encounter rules, it receives a permanent built-in `chief_navigator_domain_security_iff` hullmod and the vanilla no-automated-penalty variant tag, allowing command without Automated Ships expertise. Remnant vessels remain unaffected. Disabling the Wall's reactor permanently gives it player IFF. Across the first three Labors, recovered records point toward the Devoured Ring. Labor IV sends the player into Devoured Reach to deploy the sensor package and confirm that the Threat have spent centuries consuming the ring while trying, unsuccessfully, to reverse-engineer a Janus Device; this is why Menelaus refuses to let the player scan the gate housed inside FOB Ithaca. Reporting Labor IV opens Labor V: Gautama coordinates three perimeter Third Strikes, each with a 60-day siege clock against its own Ithaca bastion. A surviving Strike destroys that bastion at its deadline, then attacks an assigned TFS formation; TFS casualties remain lost for the rest of the Labor. The Heavenly Strike cannot materialize until all three perimeter fleets are destroyed, at which point every surviving TFS formation teleports into the center engagement. Defeating Gautama ends the siege field objective; reporting the victory completes all Labors. Reactivating and scanning the Devoured Ring unknowingly supplies the missing reciprocal topology and connects the Threat feeding ground to the Core Worlds gate lattice. Only full completion grows Ashen Verge's same six allied patrol IDs from three hulls into nine-hull armadas. Menelaus is a persistent alpha-core person displayed at Ithaca with `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_menelaus.png`. His portrait follows the vanilla AI-portrait language: a blue/cyan Corinthian helmet and Greek plume emerge only partially from a dense, unstable signal field. A broken luminous halo sits behind the helmet, while stochastic rays of varied length, spacing, intensity, and fragmentation radiate outward from his eyes like a rising sun. A restrained magenta/violet registration bloom may fringe the white-blue eyes, selected halo arcs, and sparse signal fragments, but blue/cyan remains dominant. Avoid dominant orthogonal grids, clean helmet illustration, perfect radial regularity, or physical metal.
- Defeated Guard Mothership POIs remain absent through the Labor I field phase. Committing the Labor I report causes all four to reconstruct at their exact authored coordinates as friendly Domain Combat Guard motherships. Each restored mothership maintains exactly two persistent three-ship Guard patrols, for eight total. Missing patrols respawn beside their parent mothership, travel to the Drifting Wall, and take `ORBIT_AGGRESSIVE` assignments there. Their ordinary patrol ring remains wall-centered; a fleet more than `2400` units from the Wall is returned in place to one of eight deterministic points on a `950`-unit ring, with its velocity cleared and without replacing its serialized roster. Never apply this correction while the fleet is in battle, hyperspace transition, despawning, player-controlled, or the target of an open interaction dialog. The Wall interaction must show the ordinary pre-battle fleet preview, preserve `pullInAllies = true`, and use a temporary hostile Derelict-faction combat proxy rather than a Domain Combat Guard proxy so every nearby restored patrol can appear in the preview and assist on the player's side. Keep the ten unmoored teal Guard packs in the Odyssey Expanse as vanilla-Derelict-faction hostiles. The friendly Domain Combat Guard faction and the unmoored Derelict faction must remain mutually hostile, while both populations continue to permit ordinary recovery of disabled Guard hulls.
- The Drifting Wall may be challenged whenever it remains hostile and is not rebuilding, including before the player accepts or reaches Labor III; this supersedes the older Labor-III-only assault restriction above. An early victory permanently reclaims the Wall, records Labor III as automatically complete when the preceding Labors finish, and triggers a one-time surprised reaction from Menelaus at the next Ithaca conversation before returning to the current Labor report.
- The authoritative Menelaus portrait is the exact user-supplied 124x124 RGBA image installed at `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_menelaus.png` (SHA-256 `C661C39563CB402FEA6F2496BB553AA92B7709A4BE4B00B51C349372CEB936B9`). Do not regenerate, recolor, sharpen, pad, or rescale it; this exact asset supersedes the earlier generative portrait-design guidance above.

Approximate the visible 320-degree structure with 29 overlapping invisible 185-unit collision entities along a 1,620-unit centerline radius; leave the damaged 40-degree western gap as the sole entrance. Because custom campaign-entity radii are not intrinsically solid, `FobIthacaRingCollisionScript` must also resolve every mobile fleet against that same annular arc every frame, canceling only its inward wall-crossing velocity and preserving tangential motion. In the same frame, re-pin each backing bastion fleet to its authored 2,150-unit fixed position and zero its campaign velocity: station mode and disabled AI do not prevent hostile fleet collision from translating a campaign station. Do not replace this with one center-sized collision circle, which would make Menelaus and the interior gate unreachable. The sole central interaction target is the 750-unit-radius station body. North, east, and south are persistent `CampaignFleetAPI` stations with stable IDs, station mode, independent combat fleets, and fixed positions beneath their matching baked ring modules. Keep these three station entities non-clickable and suppress only their idle flagship campaign sprites so the composite ring art remains the single visual representation; do not revert them to temporary combat proxies. The center selects which station is the objective, while the other active stations retain normal station reinforcement eligibility. A multi-station battle tiles every participating 8,000-unit emplacement and rear bulkhead into one continuous defensive line, keeps the selected station central, and persists every core destroyed during the engagement.
- FOB Ithaca contains a real vanilla inactive-gate entity with the stable id `chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_gate`, fixed in the western interior of the surviving ring. `TreadmillCampaignPlugin` must always intercept that exact entity and route it to the `ChiefNavigatorIthacaGate` rules dialog, which exposes no scan action. A persistent visible Hegemony picket named **Task Force Spartan Gate Watch** orbits the gate under a non-hostile sealed-cordon assignment; it is narrative enforcement of Menelaus's prohibition, not a second gate-combat encounter. Never allow the inactive gate's vanilla scanning dialog to become reachable at any Labor stage.
- FOB Ithaca's center uses a marketless custom station interaction that recreates colony-standard fleet, cargo, and refit access over free-transfer entity cargo, while its contact option opens Starsector's native Comm Directory through a short-lived, non-economy carrier market. Vanilla's directory constructor reads the interaction target's market despite accepting a directory argument, so attach the carrier only around the synchronous `showCommDirectoryDialog()` call and restore the prior market in `finally`; never register it with the economy or leave it attached. The station plugin must implement `RuleBasedDialog` so native person selection can run `OpenCDE` and the rules-authored `PickGreeting` flow. The north, east, and south ring modules are separate persistent `CampaignFleetAPI` stations. Each campaign fleet contains only one intact Vast Bulk foundation, is fixed at its matching campaign-art module, is directly clickable and attackable, and carries `$chief_navigator_ithaca_section_combat` so it routes into `IthacaSectionBattleCreationPlugin` instead of vanilla station combat. Its five-ship Domain Combat Guard screen is added at safe explicit combat coordinates by that plugin, not stored in the station fleet. Keep idle flagship sprites suppressed behind the baked orbital-ring art, but restore the complete 5,632x2,216 intact sprite before interaction so pulled-in station reinforcements render correctly. The encounter loader must replace older saves' `chief_navigator_ithaca_wall_segment` members with `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_base_Standard`. Do not restore the retired center-menu assault dispatch, curved wall sprite override, rear bulkheads, or separate control cores.
