Do not use clouds from `nebula_amber`, `nebula512`, `_blue`, `nebula`, `nebula_colorless`, `fx_clouds00`, or `cleaner_clouds00`, and never render a whole cloud atlas as one sprite.
- Sanzu adds a constant southward current of 30 world units per campaign advance second, equally while idle and while steering. Apply it in SilentWakeCurrentTerrain with the public fleet.setLocation setter, which synchronizes the entity and its movement module; directly editing getLocation is overwritten. Preserve propulsion velocity and active navigation targets. Carry only an already-reached idle hold point along with the fleet; a changed destination or fresh mouse input releases that hold point. Never use isFollowingDirectCommand as proof of movement: live traces show it remains true after arrival. Keep the shared white-dwarf/planet lee geometry, transition and pause guards, and idempotent save migration. Do not reintroduce idle-versus-steering force strengths or reflection.
- Sanzu's 57 baked paths guide cloud motion and sparse short directional glints; they are not separately filled visible ribbons. Do not render the former two-layer slipstream-textured mesh: its repeating longitudinal UVs create distracting horizontal stripes, and the foreground/background cloud banks now provide the continuous visual body of the current. Do not restore the older parallel solid-strip renderer either.
- Retain the analytical multi-cylinder velocity-potential helper for possible non-repeating phase effects, but do not feed repeating slipstream textures through it. The current visual is carried by cloud particles moving through the deterministic vortex-street path geometry.
- Sanzu's procedural mist must not form a regular dot or leopard-print lattice. Populate every flow path with 40 clumps whose hashed positions are not constrained to equal longitudinal slots. Use a deliberately broad, nonlinear base-size distribution of roughly 360-1,000 world units, with width never below the base size and height never below 78% of it; the system mist's minimum feature scale must not fall below the visible planet-fog feature scale. Independently vary lateral displacement, aspect, rotation, opacity, and three to seven irregular satellite lobes so the wider stream naturally produces both substantial wisps and occasional broad banks. Include a broad faint skirt around each clump so nearby smoke merges while remaining deterministic across saves.
- Sanzu also has a distinct background-depth cloud pass on `TERRAIN_1`, beneath the sparse flow highlights and the brighter main mist on `TERRAIN_2`. The custom-entity spec must register both terrain layers or the deep pass is never dispatched. It uses 20 darker, broader clumps per path, a 520-1,500 world-unit base-size range, wider lateral displacement, clearly legible blue-black opacity, and 58% of the foreground drift rate. Keep it visually subordinate without reducing it to an imperceptible same-layer underpainting.
- Sanzu's two system-scale cloud layers use independent motion phases so they can move at half the original cloud speed without slowing the textured current, sparse direction glints, planet shader, or fallback planet wisps. The brighter system mist advances at `0.006` phase units per campaign-time unit; the deep background pass retains 58% of that speed (`0.00348`).
- Sanzu's three planets have a separate GLSL 1.20 foreground-vapor pass above their sprites. It uses layered FBM density, slow curl-noise UV distortion, an analytic circular face mask, a noise-eroded elliptical upstream cap/limb, three noise-warped lower tendrils, and a soft radial integration collar to resemble dry ice flowing over a rounded vessel. The foreground pass uses one generously padded quad whose edges lie beyond every nonzero analytic mask; do not split it into overlapping circular and wake meshes, since their entrance and overlap boundaries produce visible straight and curved lines. Do not restore the separable rectangular cap mask, the minimum-density haze term, or any rectangular boundary multiplier: all visible alpha must come from FBM density or filaments, leaving the padded quad completely transparent outside the organic masks. The lower tendrils begin a noise-perturbed opacity falloff at roughly 2.7 planet radii downstream and square that falloff before becoming fully transparent near 5 radii; this long uneven dissolution prevents the trail from reading as a distinct attached object. Its colors must remain in the surrounding stream's blue palette rather than approaching white; its fine feature scale is the visual reference for the wider stream, not something to enlarge to match the old oversized clumps. The `chief_navigator_silent_wake_flow` custom-entity spec must stay registered on both `TERRAIN_2` and `ABOVE_STATIONS`; otherwise Starsector never dispatches the foreground shader branch. Preserve the procedural ellipse implementation only as the automatic shader-failure fallback; the star does not receive either foreground pass.
- A GraphicsLib `RippleDistortion` is automatically removed as soon as its animation frame reaches `60`, regardless of a long configured lifetime. Charybdis's maintained field must use `frameRate = 0`, a fixed safe `currentFrame` below `60`, fixed intensity, and a 1,000-unit base radius that becomes 4,000 units after its one-time 50%-hull enrage. The prototype uses the complete stock Shrouded Maw renderer, including its WobblyParts and smoke, scaled to 150% and blue-shifted by adding 36% of each source color's red channel to its blue channel through a custom Maw creator. Do not clear the plugin parts or force the hidden underlying hull sprite visible.
- Charybdis has 1.4x base hull and 0.5x base armor, and the distortion supplies its encounter rules. Its built-in Pseudoparticle Manifold replaces only its two stock jets with dedicated variants that are 4x as wide and deal 2.5x damage. Its six dedicated Hungering Rift launchers fire missiles with 2x visual/collision dimensions and 1.5x missile hitpoints. The first time it reaches 50% hull, play the original harsh, inharmonic Budai screech and force four rapid Convulsive Lunges in random directions over independently random distances. Only after the fourth lunge, expand the current distortion radius to 4x base, overlay a restrained red tint within that circle, and halve only those Rift launchers' refire delay; do not double its other weapons. Projectile spawn points and live beam origins outside the current radius deal 50% damage to Charybdis and raise a rate-limited `DEFLECTED!` impact message, except that high-explosive hits against its armor or hull always deal normal full damage because their detonation is treated as expanding from inside the bubble. Every ship inside receives a 50% modifier to speed, acceleration, deceleration, turn speed, and turn acceleration; hostile ships are also struck by periodic Shrouded Lens arcs. Do not replace this with the fallback global outgoing-damage buff unless the direct shot-origin listener stops functioning against a future Starsector API.
- Budai retains the stock Maw's offensive Convulsive Lunge behavior through its dedicated variable-distance system and has no combat healing or regeneration mechanic. It never retreats or receives the retired low-hull cooldown penalty. While a hostile non-fighter is close and in front, its dedicated system AI adds a 900-unit backward lunge about every six unpaused combat seconds, respecting normal activation, flux, cooldown, and map-edge constraints. These backward lunges yield to the four random lunges in the one-shot 50% hull enrage sequence. At exactly 50% hull or below during advancing combat, immediately switch its looping Heart of Corruption opening to Lappy's The Savor of Tomorrow, reusing the existing Ungaikyo audio asset. The switch is irreversible for that battle, retries once per cue, and returns music to the campaign afterward; ordinary Gautama reincarnations retain Heart of Corruption. At the start of enrage, triple only Budai's native maintained shroud pool and quadruple its normal and overloaded smoke counts, widening spread by 35% and extending smoke duration by 25%. Apply these per-ship multipliers once, retry if the native shroud is not yet available, and retain native smoke textures, colors, movement, and cleanup.
- Battles against the actual Budai flagship loop **Heart of Corruption** by Lappy through `BudaiBattleCreationPlugin`; Gautama's own live hull also owns that cue outside the protected final-Labor soundtrack. Never apply it to ordinary Shrouded Dwellers or other THREAT ships without Gautama. Labor V's perimeter and center-breach combat retain their instrumental or vocal **My Gospel is Gunpowder** cues. Always preserve explicit Lappy credit and the source URL in both the README and audio-source record when distributing the bundled track.
- Avici campaign music uses the vanilla Shrouded Dweller encounter cue `music_dweller_encounter_hostile`, looping on entry and resuming after combat. Store this exact cue in Avici's native `$musicSetId` so replacement music-player plugins honor it; do not restore the timed KARMA intro/A/B playlist or copy/override vanilla audio. Keep native encounter/location music priority and release the custom stream on leaving. Budai's combat soundtrack and other systems are unchanged. Retain inactive KARMA assets and zippy's source credit.
- Sanzu campaign music loops zippy's `Watcher of the Cycle -Scene02- (NoVox)` from the official June 2026 YouTube-members download. Assign it through Sanzu's native `$musicSetId`; do not restore the one-shot campaign script. Combat may take precedence, and Starsector's location music must resume automatically afterward. Runtime music assets must be real Ogg Vorbis. Preserve zippy's credit and the public source-video URL in both the README and audio-source record.
- Budai is a one-time vengeance-style hunter confined to Avici. Use a direct lookup of his owned fleet ID in Avici, a spawn flag, and the durable `$chief_navigator_budai_defeated_v1` flag; never respawn him. Record defeat when Budai is actually absent from the owned fleet's post-battle roster or the fleet receives `DESTROYED_BY_BATTLE`, not merely because the player disengaged. Retire the original fleet and its remaining escorts after battle/dialog/transition activity ends; always exclude player fleets. No legacy timer conversion, reward-based defeat inference, global recovery search, duplicate reconciliation, or old-variant repair is required. Budai tracks the player's exact live position only while both fleets are in Avici, otherwise returns to his lair near Kshaya, and cannot use jump points. Avici's fog must not slow him or break pursuit. The player-involved defeat queues one rules-authored salvage scene whose extraction grants exactly two `chief_navigator_mountable_heavy_adjudicator` weapons with Isa or one without her and commits `$chief_navigator_budai_heavy_adjudicators_granted_v1`. Show the consumed Onslaught Mk.I only as a temporary scene fleet-member visual and cut it apart completely; never create a persistent wreck or recoverable hull. The weapon clones vanilla `heavy_adjudicator` art, projectile, sound, firing behavior, ammo, OP cost, and combat statistics. Remove only the `SYSTEM` hint and `H_A_only` group restriction, use a 500,000-credit base value, and exclude it from blueprints, production, dealers, random drops, and salvage drops. Isa helps only when Hall of Triumph's durable recruitment flag has been observed, with no Hall class dependency. If Budai remains undefeated after Labor V, add his authored Dweller escort once; a defeated Budai never returns.
- Once the player first enters any Odyssey Sector system, begin the region's stranded-human timeline. Sanzu has no hostile Guard patrols, Starving Threat, or Budai. Completing Labor I causes one large, personally hostile **Persean League Survivors** fleet to arrive there, centered on a Pegasus and supported by a Champion, two Eagles, a Gryphon, Falcons, Eradicators, destroyers, and frigates. Give every hull D-mods and damaged CR, keep the Pegasus less degraded than its escorts, force the formation to intercept the player without formal League reputation impact, prevent it from leaving Sanzu, and never respawn it after defeat. Preserve a canonical fleet that already arrived in an older save; replace only the former procgen League population with this authored composition. Sanzu also supports at most two small Persean exploration patrols: each has one or two combat escorts plus one Shepherd, Wayfarer, or Buffalo, remains non-hostile, and cannot leave Sanzu. Completing Labor III causes one battered Hegemony expedition to arrive in Alpha Odyssey and establish its persistent forward base at `(-6500, 4800)`. Require the first three Labors—the four Guard Motherships, delivery and installation of four distinct Ithaca defense components, and the Drifting Wall—to be complete; the other two stations and components remain optional and recoverable. The expedition is mostly logistics hulls, with many frigates, several Enforcers, a few cruisers, and exactly one Legion XIV; that Legion remains battered but uniquely has the maximum three S-mods. It is personally hostile regardless of the player's formal Hegemony relationship, hunts the player in Alpha Odyssey, and demands 60 supplies plus 120 fuel "for the good of humanity." Payment grants fourteen days of safe passage from both Voss's expedition and all three Foxtrot Terminus patrols; refusal or inability to pay starts combat without a harmless pre-battle leave option. Neither outcome changes formal Hegemony reputation, and the expedition does not respawn after destruction. Maintain exactly three small, personally hostile Foxtrot Terminus patrols after the expedition arrives. They orbit aggressively, cannot leave Alpha Odyssey, ignore formal Hegemony relations, and cause no faction reputation loss outside an active tribute-grace period. Their forward base and patrol cordon obstruct the player's ability to leave the Orion Knot unmolested. Only after this expedition's persistent arrival flag is set may at most two Hegemony exile patrols appear in Sanzu. Voss confiscates supplies, arbitrarily recalculates rations in the name of collective need, and sends malcontents into dangerous, barren reaches of the Orion Knot. He then treats their meager returns and predictable failures as further offenses until he can exile them. Exile fleets contain two or three frigates with a 30% chance of one outdated Enforcer, carry one or two D-mods per hull at 32-59% CR, and remain confined to Sanzu. Each rolls a posture lasting four to nine days: 45% of the time it becomes personally hostile and aggressive without always-pursue behavior, and otherwise it becomes non-hostile and actively avoids the player. Both small Sanzu patrol populations use randomized 18-32 day replacement intervals and never exceed two live fleets apiece. Admiral Oren Voss is a communist satire/critique: he dresses armed confiscation in language of collective ownership, voluntary contribution, arbitrary ration calculations, and the **Good of humanity** while steadily losing his grip on reality. His recurring requisitions are intentionally one of the pressures that makes casual movement into and out of the Odyssey Sector difficult.
- After that expedition arrives, play one save-persistent command-conference scene. Voss naively attempts to assume control of the entire FOB Ithaca macrocomplex under a claim of Fourteenth Battlegroup continuity. Menelaus refuses and cites his standing strategic authority as **Strategos**; Captain Aias Kleon remains Ithaca's acting Base Commander, and Task Force Spartan aligns with Menelaus. Voss lashes out and calls Menelaus a machine, a core, and merely a tool. Menelaus loses his composure, answers exactly and emphatically **"I AM HUMAN!"**, and casts the Hegemony officer out of Ithaca's command channel. The officer is publicly humiliated, but his expedition remains at its Alpha Odyssey forward base and turns to armed requisitions and harassment of the Orion Knot approaches.
- Menelaus's displayed Task Force Spartan rank is **Strategos**, using the dedicated `chief_navigator_strategos` faction-rank id, and his non-administrative post is **Strategic Intelligence**. Reapply both whenever his persistent person is loaded so older saves migrate away from the former **Admiral / Base Commander** identity and vanilla colony-management options cannot pollute his Labors menu. Captain Aias Kleon owns and continuously reapplies Ithaca's **Base Commander** post instead.
- Do not add `module_no_status_bar` to Charybdis. That tag hides the large targeting outline, but it also suppresses the locked-target hull and flux readout; preserving actionable combat information takes priority. Starsector exposes these as one vanilla target-UI behavior, not two independent skin options.
- Every Starving Threat base hull installs only `chief_navigator_starving_threat`, a complete replacement for stock `threat_hullmod`. It extends the stock implementation and retains the shared Threat combat stat package, but removes stock's zero-profile multiplier, applies +200% sensor profile, marks the ship against stock reclamation, and replaces that death outcome with attack swarms. Never install both hullmods on one Starving hull. Gautama's skin removes the custom hullmod and its variant must not equip `fragment_swarm` or stock `threat_hullmod`.
- Scylla inherits from the dedicated `chief_navigator_scylla_base` hull. Its 570x555 sprite, `(285,199.5)` pivot, collision bounds, shield, engines, and weapon slots are all authored directly at 150% scale; do not reintroduce runtime sprite/spec scaling. The ordinary and Labor V `chief_navigator_scylla` skin has exactly twenty fighter bays and twenty repeated `chief_navigator_scylla_attack_swarm_wing` built-in wings, establishing a hard twenty-swarm live cap with an unlimited five-second carrier refit. The retired `chief_navigator_scylla_final` zero-bay skin remains only for data and old-save compatibility and is not the Devoured Reach encounter. Every historical wing contains one stock-derived Attack Swarm carrying `chief_navigator_scylla_fragile_swarm`: 25% hull, 150% hull and armor damage taken, no underlying-ship explosion or hulk. Preserve ordinary damageability, omit the former fragment-chain renderer, keep `chief_navigator_scylla_invincible_swarm` inert for save compatibility, and never make the stock `attack_swarm` hull globally invulnerable.
- Starving Threat keeps normal top speed but has 5x acceleration, deceleration, turn acceleration, and maximum turn rate, 0.67x hull, and 1.15x armor. On death the custom hullmod suppresses stock reclamation, launches attack swarms, and in ordinary unsuppressed battles may begin Gautama's one-per-battle THREAT construction: 10% for a frigate, 20% for a destroyer, 30% for a cruiser, and 50% for the Fabricator. Starving Hive Units now use their native `TIMID` support personality and complete vanilla fit, geometry and Hive fragment reserve/regeneration bonuses; enforce the doctrine on campaign/fabricated Hives without changing other roles. This supersedes the former aggressive doctrine and Hive fragment exclusions.
- Avici contains no THREAT fleets or rogue-derelict flotillas; Budai is its only roaming hostile fleet. Ashen Verge maintains up to ten Ithaca-focused Starving Threat patrols, with a save-persistent three-campaign-day replacement delay after each defeat. It also materializes one transient First or Second Strike 11,000–15,000 units from Ithaca every 25–35 campaign days; this raid attacks one surviving bastion through Nexerelin's hidden-target station-assault sequence (`ATTACK_LOCATION` on an invisible 100-unit orbit token, then `GO_TO_LOCATION`, then `INTERCEPT`) and keeps the real bastion as its tactical priority target. It remains mutually hostile with the player so the player can join Ithaca's defense, and disappears after a twenty-day attack window so a survivor cannot block later monthly raids. The `SpawnSTInvasionFleets` console command is a test-only exception: it creates three full Third Strike fleets in Ashen Verge, assigning one persistent Nex-style assault to each of Ithaca's north, east, and south bastions outside Labor V. Command-spawned invasion ships and the canonical final-Labor invaders carry a hidden compatibility marker used only for battle-side discovery; it changes no combat statistics. Devoured Reach maintains twenty-four renewable three-group feeding masses: sixteen orbit aggressively against the ring and eight orbit the hollow worlds. None patrol the system or the hyperspace side, and its sole Threshold maintains a clear arrival bubble. Labor V's four-wave invasion remains inside Ashen Verge. Its first three fleets hold at separate standoff tokens instead of entering campaign autoresolve, cannot damage their bastions before their individual 60-day deadlines, permanently destroy those bastions at expiry, and then attack the north mouth guard, gate watch, and south mouth guard respectively (falling back to another surviving TFS fleet). Do not replace or repair TFS ships during Labor V. Count a perimeter wave only when its invasion fleet is actually destroyed, regardless of whether the player or TFS wins the battle. Spawn the Gautama center fleet only after all three perimeter fleets are dead, give it thirty-three supporting hulls in three staged Fabricator/Hive/escort waves, and teleport every surviving TFS formation directly onto it exactly once. Destroying the center fleet must set a durable return-to-Menelaus state, immediately stop Labor V's invasion controller, and keep the fifth Labor and Ithaca gate incomplete until the player finishes Menelaus's FOB Ithaca debrief. Closing that debrief queues a separate, one-time Hall-style 480×300 illustrated completion interaction; open it only after every other dialog, menu, and core tab has closed, and persist both pending and shown flags. Apply `MEMORY_KEY_NO_JUMP` to every THREAT fleet. Starving Threat fleets are manually populated autonomous swarms: disable their fleet inflater, migrate non-Ashen fleets and their serialized commanders from Omega to vanilla Threat identity in place, keep Ashen fleets on the dedicated Starving faction with Threat portrait and presentation, clear patrol status, ignore player comms, and use Threat-derived no-comm fleet interaction so they never show human patrol dialogue. Preserve existing fleet, member, officer, and skill objects during this migration. On load and each population update, delete non-quest THREAT fleets found in hyperspace and return Gautama to the Devoured Ring except while Labor V's center-breach fleet is active in Ashen Verge. Never recreate a defeated Ashen patrol on the next update.
- Command-siege persistence must compensate for campaign autoresolve, which does not run combat damage listeners. If autoresolve eliminates a `SpawnSTInvasionFleets` lane before the player participates, re-form its fleet and resume the same bastion assault. Once the player participates in that lane's battle, disable replacement there so the player's attrition and victory remain permanent.
- Each command invasion is exclusively locked to its assigned north/east/south bastion. Continuously set both the tactical priority and active target to that station, tell the invasion AI not to attack the player, TFS fleets, other bastions, or any unrelated fleet, and reciprocally tell non-player fleets not to intercept the invasion. Preserve hostile relations so the player may still voluntarily join the bastion battle.
- Avici also contains the doomed bombarded world Kshaya and its orbital habitat, originally built for 52,000 people but reduced to 87 cannibal worshippers of Budai. Sinni insists on joining the evacuation delegation, guarded by the player's two best bodyguards, Wei and Yvan. A mother and her young son secretly defect; rescuing them triggers a cult ambush and reduces the habitat's population to 85 before any ambush casualties. The player may bombard the habitat or withdraw. If spared, the habitat is warm, pressurized, and empty on the next interaction, with its final record implying that Budai consumed everyone left aboard. Preserve the two rescued passengers as a global story flag rather than ordinary cargo crew. Historical internal IDs retain `naraka` solely for save compatibility; every player-facing name is Kshaya.
- Ashen Verge patrols use the dedicated chief_navigator_ithaca_threat faction: hostile to both the player and Domain Combat Guard. Explicit assignments normally intercept surviving Ithaca bastions; no blanket MAKE_AGGRESSIVE or ALWAYS_PURSUE flags. Outside 3,500 units from Ithaca only, a patrol may briefly redirect from its siege target when it sees the player within 700 units and has at least 1.3x the player fleet points: 10% chance per eligible check, at most once per three campaign days, lasting up to 0.2 days. Losing sight, exceeding the proximity limit, or entering the refuge immediately cancels pursuit and restores the bastion assignment. IthacaSupportInteraction allows the nearest available, player-friendly wall module hostile to the opponent to support player encounters from 2,500 units (edge-to-edge), without globally expanding other fleets' join range or moving the campaign station.
- Starving Threat's governing theme is ephemerality, short-livedness, and mortality. Its archived bone-weapon prototypes express this through ammunition, longevity, and self-damage drawbacks, but active Starving fleets now use unmodified stock THREAT weapons. Do not restore the prototype Skullblaster or its self-damaging feedback to active loadouts.
- Starvation makes Starving Threat hulls ravenous and unconcerned with stealth. Encode this on the replacement `chief_navigator_starving_threat` hullmod itself as a +200% sensor-profile modifier (3x total profile), after explicitly removing the inherited stock zero-profile modifier, so fleet visibility scales naturally with its Starving hull composition. Do not use a fixed fleet-level visibility override or apply the bonus to Gautama and ordinary THREAT hulls.
- Starving Threat recovers zero supplies and uses stock THREAT weapon IDs. The inactive `chief_navigator_bone_*` definitions and `tools/generate_starving_threat_weapons.ps1` remain archived prototype material; generating those definitions must not put them back into fleet variants. Their historical half-OP costs and drawbacks do not apply to the active stock weapons.
- Non-Hive ordinary Starving Threat loadouts must not equip fragment-dependent weapons (`swarm_launcher`, `seeker_fragment`, `kinetic_fragments`, `unstable_fragment`, `devouring_swarm`, or `voltaic_discharge`). Their shared fragment economy is deliberately excluded as too complicated to balance for this faction variant. Gautama's fixed boss loadouts remain the authored exception.
- The Starving Fabricator uses `chief_navigator_starving_construction_swarm`, maps every constructable stock THREAT variant to its Starving counterpart, and subtracts half the normal construction CR cost from the Fabricator itself. Spawned units keep normal starting CR; do not apply a 50% CR penalty to them.
- Waypoint Troy has a separate persistent departure entity, `chief_navigator_troy_anchorage`, backed by an unlocked storage submarket. Departure must start only through `TroyDepartureInteraction`, never merely because the player entered the system. Sum active non-fighter fleet members' live deployment-point costs and block wormhole transit above 50 DP in both directions; recheck at the Troy entrance and Foxtrot Terminus Aperture at the instant of transition so storage transfers cannot leave a stale permission state. Leaving either custom wormhole without transiting must mirror vanilla jump-point exit handling: set campaign-UI following-direct-command state, unpause the sector, then dismiss the dialog so an active plotted course cannot immediately retarget the aperture.
- Never use `createSectorMap` for the Orion Knot chart: the native widget clamps its viewport to vanilla hyperspace bounds and cannot show the Knot at its remote coordinates. The Adept of the Stars and Waves Intel embeds no chart. After arrival, Sinni's contact launches a full-size custom-dialog chart that reads the six real authored `StarSystemAPI` coordinates and normalizes their relative positions into local panel space. Match the user's vanilla sector-map reference with stock planet-spec map icons and their native UI colors, stock blue map-nebula atlas cells sampled read-only from the actual hyperspace terrain, and the restrained blue square grid. Keep cleaned names, inset labels, the north reference, broken plotted traversal legs, and the regional Abyss label. Cache only panel-visible cloud samples when opening the chart; never render the whole atlas or advance/edit terrain. Plot the fleet only while it is within the Knot using the same coordinate transform as the systems. This is a live bounds-independent UI renderer, not a static image and not another Intel entry.
- The abandoned low-tech station beside Old Milix is player-facing as the **Domain Derelict Battlestation**. Its rules-authored inspection follows physical evidence through the outer hull, habitation decks, and command archive: the Fourteenth methodically disarmed it, moved its stores and components into the Abyssal departure column, and evacuated the crew rather than losing the station in battle. The conclusion grants no salvage or mechanical reward. Preserve the stable entity ID and serialized token, and stop intercepting the derelict inspection after the same entity becomes the Hegemony Expeditionary Forward Base.
- Every authored mobile Derelict fleet uses the teal Domain Combat Guard skins, is confined to realspace, carries `MEMORY_KEY_NO_JUMP`, and permits ordinary recovery of disabled Guard hulls. Friendly restored-mothership and Ashen Verge patrols use the Domain Combat Guard faction; hostile unmoored Labor roamers use the vanilla Derelict faction so they attack the restored patrols. The one-time Rogue Combat Guard flotillas retain their encounter-specific faction behavior. Migrate stock-colored Rampart, Bastillon, Berserker, Defender, Picket, Sentry, and Warden members in existing saves to their matching Combat Guard variants in place. Rogue flotillas never respawn after defeat and must never materialize within 7,000 units of the player. Their fixed anchors must remain clear of all four Guard Mothership Labor sites; migrate older serialized flotillas to the corrected layout once, but never move a fleet in battle, transition, or an active interaction. On load and population updates, relocate escaped legacy fleets from hyperspace to their assigned home system and remove duplicate or temporary combat-proxy fleets. Refresh native navigation avoidance around every in-system jump point for those four mobile families without clearing their assignments, tactical targets, or vanilla avoidance. Keep Rogue patrol anchors at least 7,500 units from every jump point by moving the existing hidden anchor token in place. If an intact mobile patrol enters the 4,500-unit jump-arrival danger radius, move that same serialized fleet beyond 6,500 units from every overlapping zone while preserving its roster, assignments, targets, orbiting state, and velocity; never delete or replace it to make the entry safe, never move a fleet in battle, transition, fixed orbit, or an active interaction, and do not reposition a fleet already outside the danger radius.
- FOB Ithaca's central entity must own no persistent `MarketAPI`. On loading a save from the retired market-backed implementation, move all storage cargo and mothballed ships back into the station's entity cargo, detach the authored residents, remove procedural market NPCs, unregister `chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_storage_market` from the economy, clear the station market, and enable free transfer without loss. The native Comm Directory may use only an unregistered carrier market temporarily attached for the duration of its constructor call and detached immediately afterward. The one-time approach scene ends with native DismissDialog. The next direct interaction with Ithaca's central station opens the full Menelaus introduction; approach completion must not retarget its interaction or open the introduction automatically. An interrupted introduction remains pending for the next central-station interaction. Only after the introduction should the custom station interaction own Ithaca's storage, fleet transfer, refit, and native comm-directory entry point. Populate that directory in authored order with Menelaus, Captain Aias Kleon, and rescued residents; never include Sinni. The Kshaya rescue permanently adds Mira Sen and her son Toma as authored residents, with Mira as the directory contact; their dialogue remembers Sara only when she died during the rescue. Every later Menelaus briefing, report, Labor hub, upgrade, and free-repair dialogue is entered through **Discuss the Labors.** on Menelaus's contact; his dialogue must never offer recursive station-directory or station-services options, and leaving it returns to the native directory. The Odyssey Sector maintains sparse, one-time hostile Rogue Combat Guard Flotillas only in the Odyssey Expanse, Ashen Verge, and Last Light; Sanzu, Avici, and Devoured Reach contain none. Unlike boss and Starving populations, Combat Guard fleets must not set `MEMORY_KEY_NO_SHIP_RECOVERY`, because their disabled hulls are an important finite source of replacements while the expedition is isolated.
- The Odyssey Expanse, Sanzu, and Ashen Verge each contain exactly two fixed vanilla `station_research_remnant` salvage POIs. Generate them once per system behind `$chief_navigator_odyssey_research_stations_v1`; the marker must remain set after salvage so loading or re-entering the system never recreates their loot.
- Populate every ordinary Odyssey destination with dead worlds, frequent ruins, and one-time salvageable orbital-habitat and mining-station remnants. Ashen Verge is the densest graveyard: all seven of its authored worlds have ruins and it contains nine non-research salvage sites. Sanzu retains exactly its three flow-obstacle planets; add ruins to Cairn and Undertow instead of adding more worlds there. Keep Kshaya Habitat separate from Avici's generic remnants. Devoured Reach must contain no ordinary worlds, habitats, or mining stations—only Mara, Vey, Ketu, the Devoured Ring, and its Threat population.
- Odyssey Sector research stations never drop vanilla or custom AI cores. Remove their default `ai_cores*` salvage groups, strip legacy guaranteed `chief_navigator_odyssey_combat_core` cargo from surviving stations, and provide only the deterministic Ithaca defense component in addition to their ordinary non-core salvage. Guarantee all six authored stations by reconciling every unsalvaged station ID independently; the per-system generation marker must never suppress repair of one missing station. Record vanilla salvage with a durable per-station flag rather than inferring completion from an absent entity. Every surviving station remains permanently visible, labeled, and marked on its Tab system map, using a marker reason independent of the active Labor marker. Retain the obsolete core commodity, officer plugin, and skill definitions only so old saves deserialize safely; never register the campaign plugin that makes new copies assignable.
- Entering Waypoint Troy may announce and mark the anchorage, but must never lay in a course, enable autopilot, or otherwise move the player's fleet toward it. The player approaches and interacts with the anchorage manually.
- The obsolete standalone hostile Task Force Spartan test fleet no longer spawns in Alpha Odyssey/Odyssey Expanse. On load and during population reconciliation, remove only fleets bearing its historical `chief_navigator_task_force_spartan_test` ID or marker, deferring removal while one is in battle, hyperspace transition, or an active interaction. Preserve FOB Ithaca's three Spartan cordon fleets; they use separate IDs and are the story presence of Task Force Spartan. Retain the historical sample ID, marker, and battle plugin only for old-save cleanup and an already-running encounter.
- The eventual TFS story encounter supports either a player victory or defeat. Both branches must end in peaceful de-escalation rather than exterminating Spartan or establishing permanent hostility; combat outcome changes the framing of the reconciliation, not whether reconciliation occurs.
- The Spartan Phantom deploys one built-in two-craft `chief_navigator_boarding_pod_wing`. These are real fighters, not missile projectiles: their custom AI holds a close defensive orbit around the source Phantom and commits only after detecting an enemy hull not covered by an active shield arc. On contact the fighter becomes an attached shuttle rendered with the direct-render `-90` degree correction, follows the target's local armor position, displays `Boarded!`, and drains 0.01 CR per second for exactly 10 seconds. The deployed and attached representations both preserve the source shuttle's 18:22 aspect and off-center 9,9 pivot (scaled to 36x44 and 18,18). Resolve the impact by tracing `ShipAPI.isPointInBounds()` from the pod toward the target and refining the first outside-to-inside crossing; never attach at `getCollisionRadius()`, which is only a broad-phase circle and floats away from long/narrow sprites. Boarding is explicitly registered as a fighter loss, and the bay replaces pods on a 15-second refit cycle.

- The damaged Wall launcher sockets use `[60,1316]`, `[60,-1316]`, `[-272,988]`, `[-272,-988]`. The intact Ithaca artwork has different socket positions: `[68,1460]`, `[68,-1460]`, `[-268,1104]`, `[-268,-1104]`; author these directly in chief_navigator_ithaca_foundation.ship, the dedicated base of the existing Ithaca skin. Preserve the skin ID and variant IDs for saves. Skin JSON ignores location overrides. Never use reflection; Starsector blocks it at runtime. Keep the naval gun sockets unchanged. The rear reactor's collision mass is 2,000, not 150,000; its hull, armor, hitbox, and role as the destructible victory target remain intact.
- The hostile Drifting Wall has no Gargoyles or timed reinforcement waves. Preserve its existing eight-ship Domain Combat Guard escort and collision-safe rear spawn positions, then release all eight to native free-moving Fearless combat AI. Never replace their normal AI with fixed reactor posts, pursuit suppression, or scripted braking: native AI must own pursuit, weapons, venting, and ship-system use. Every Domain Combat Guard hull everywhere in the mod and the Last Light Guardian use vanilla-style Fearless behavior: Reckless personality, offensive strafing, no ordinary backing off, no undamaged-armor facing bias, and unrestrained burn-drive use. The foundation/reactor mechanics and sole-reactor victory condition remain unchanged.
- Each of the six Odyssey research stations drops one deterministic special component. Labor II progress is the number of distinct components delivered to Menelaus and installed in Ithaca's defense wall, and the Labor requires any four; all six stations and components remain individually tracked and recoverable, with the final two optional. Station count must never act as progress or as an ordinal upgrade level. In station order these are `chief_navigator_ithaca_macroservos` (quadruple colossal naval-turret traverse), `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_cycler` (naval cooldowns at 40% of base), `chief_navigator_ithaca_arc_projector` (shield-respecting, EMP-only arcs to enemies within 350 units of the beam, at 200 EMP per 0.25 seconds), `chief_navigator_ithaca_intercept_matrix` (activate four fixed fortress-scale Cloudswarm PD launchers on each intact Ithaca foundation), `chief_navigator_ithaca_munitions_compiler` (convert each base Hammer torpedo into a guided Atropos while retaining ten-shot salvos and station range), and `chief_navigator_ithaca_autoloader_core` (missile refire/reload at 40% of base and expand every Cloudswarm burst from 41 to 81). Components remain inert in player cargo until transferred to Menelaus at FOB Ithaca; installation consumes one physical component and permanently records its independent bit. Menelaus's ledger must distinguish installed, recovered-awaiting-installation, and missing components. Existing saves receive matching physical items for previously salvaged station IDs, while a one-time migration preserves masks from the old auto-unlock behavior and consumes the matching carried item once. Apply the resulting component mask to `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_base` weapons, every `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_mount` child, and the `chief_navigator_spartan_battlestation` parent used by the Gate Defense Station and its two final-battle reserves. The Cloudswarm launchers fire replenishing 41-missile salvos, or 81 with the Autoloader Core, whose guidance may target only hostile missiles, fighters, and combat drones, prioritizing missiles, and never ordinary ships. Preserve the stock Locust's alternating `-2, +1, +2, -1` tube-angle cadence across all 54 cells, the `DO_NOT_AIM` hint, and a 0.18-second 120-speed off-axis ejection phase followed by a smooth 0.32-second turn-and-acceleration transition to 520 speed; custom guidance must not snap missiles directly onto trajectory at spawn. If Hall of Triumph's durable `$ship_trophy_room_isa_officer_granted` flag has ever been set, persist Chief Navigator's own unlock and give eligible Ithaca naval beams 50% additional native shield damage plus a half-strength damaging refraction to one other enemy every 0.25 seconds of shield contact; the secondary EMP-arc implementation must respect shields and must never propagate. The colossal naval beam is a `STRIKE` weapon whose authored `pierceSet` includes `FIGHTER`, so fighter wings and combat drones such as Fragment Swarms are ignored as targets and impact bodies. Against non-fighters, naval beams must retain ordinary Starsector targeting, collision, and native damage behavior: do not override their ship collision classes, force their aim or fire state, pierce allied ship hulls, or replace their damage in script. Snapshot the component mask and Isa unlock once per eligible ship deployment and clone only per-weapon specs; never globally modify stock weapons or strengthen the hostile Wall through player upgrades. `MaxUpgradeWallModules` is a campaign-only testing command that supplies a persistent all-components override without adding items, deleting research POIs, or advancing Labor II. Keep the Labors Intel and item descriptions synchronized.

- Death-spawned Starving Attack Swarms must use the wreck's getOriginalOwner(), never getOwner() (hulks become neutral owner 100). Keep spawned Attack Swarm wings in fleet-manager tracking without a dead carrier source. Initialize members like the stock SwarmLauncherEffect: hide the carrier sprite, suppress explosions/hulks, clear armor component maps, and create their FragmentSwarmHullmod cloud. Do not substitute a visible solid drone for the fragment swarm. While any real ship from their combat side remains deployed, the swarms fight normally. If detached Attack Swarms become that side's only deployed units, remove those wings immediately so they cannot consume the active deployment slot and hold Starving Threat reserves off the field or delay the battle's conclusion.

- Reducing reactor mass alone did not resolve lethal collision damage. Both the Wall foundation and rear reactor now install chief_navigator_wall_contact_safety (inherited by Ithaca). Its source-scoped DamageDealtModifier zeros null-context body impact/structural blast damage against ships, while preserving beam, projectile, and EMP contexts. Keep physical collision classes, hitboxes, and incoming weapon damage intact; do not replace this with global collision settings or player damage immunity.

- `GautamaReincarnation` owns the ordinary one-per-battle size-based reconstruction roll, Labor V's repeatable +10-per-failed-roll pity cycle, and the shared `REINCARNATING!` THREAT construction effect. Preserve all historical public/static entry points and state keys. `suppressForBattle()` must disable the roll for Labor V's perimeter battles and the permanent Ungaikyo encounter; `enableFinalLaborMode()` owns the center battle's repeatable cycles. A reconstructed Gautama uses the swarm-free `chief_navigator_scylla_reincarnating_Fabricator` combat variant, then stages exactly twenty detached Gautama Attack Swarms at one per 1.25 unpaused combat seconds after construction completes; never restore twenty launch-ready carrier bays to that reconstruction variant. The ordinary campaign Gautama retains its carrier-driven screen. The Ungaikyo encounter controller separately reuses the vanilla THREAT construction animation for fleet reflections without invoking Gautama's reconstruction path.

- Gautama uses the stock Line Unit `extraction_protocol` system and retains the `threat_overseer` role tag. Preserve the historical SYSTEM slots in its base hull geometry for data/save compatibility even though Extraction Protocol does not require Energy Lash emitters. Never restore the construction-swarmed ship system or `threat_fabricator` role to Gautama. Its variants must not equip Fragment Coordinator or Secondary Fabricator. The historical Scylla/Fabricator variant identifier remains for save compatibility. Ungaikyo is the separate stock-derived Fabricator used only by the post-Labor encounter.

- Drifting Wall campaign visibility uses null sensor/discoverable overrides, normal sprite blending, an `850 x 334.305` advertised footprint, and a `450`-unit interaction radius. The three FOB Ithaca bastion interaction tokens use the same `450`-unit radius. Hegemony arrival and its Ithaca command scene unlock only after the player reports Labor III to Menelaus; this necessarily includes the reported mothership and research Labors. Existing expeditions are preserved.

- Every BaseTerrain subclass must override getActiveLayers, getRenderRange, and getEffectCategory: their base implementations throw at runtime. SilentWakeCurrentTerrain declares no visual layers (the flow entity renders its clouds) and a 25,000-unit range, also used by BaseTerrain for fleet-effect dispatch. Verify registration callbacks and BaseTerrain.advance, not just applyEffect.

- Sanzu movement verification must include the real SmoothMovementModule between impulses. The old half-acceleration implementation produced zero net travel despite passing velocity-only mocks. tools/SilentWakeMovementRegression.java checks idle travel, campaign-step scaling, upstream controls, and shelter using direct engine calls only in the external test harness; runtime code stays on public APIs and contains no reflection.

- The sixteen ordinary Odyssey Sector jump points on the hyperspace side are open and map-marked. Keep three in-system points asymmetric for every system except Devoured Reach, which keeps only its single Threshold. Generate each three-point system's hyperspace endpoint offsets from a stable per-system seed with independently varied angular gaps and radii, so save/reload never moves them but neighboring systems never repeat one radial or triangular template; Avici alone constrains that scatter to an irregular inward-facing fan so its apertures remain clear of the Abyss. Fifteen other ordinary endpoints vary deterministically in radius; preserve Avici's primary `350` interaction radius and remove its obsolete custom gravity-well companion. Foxtrot Terminus Aperture inside Alpha Odyssey is the sole nonstandard aperture. All ordinary in-system jump points lead only to their paired hyperspace entrances; remove every stale destination into another realspace system on every load. Preserve exactly one stable vanilla `NascentGravityWellAPI` for the primary star of each of the five starred systems and remove duplicate autogenerated wells; starless Devoured Reach has none. A versioned repair must recreate stale serialized wells once and immediately place each at its system anchor, rather than trusting a canonical ID whose renderer or auto-location binding may be broken. Call `generateAnchorIfNeeded()` for all six Odyssey systems, align each canonical `system_anchor` with the system's repaired hyperspace coordinates, and clear its sensor profile, discoverability override, finite detected range, and extended-range fade so every system marker remains rendered from anywhere in the pocket. Give the three Devoured Reach hollow-world entities the same planet-style permanent visibility on every load. Their dedicated `HollowWorldEntityPlugin` returns a 30,000-unit render range because the stock `BaseCustomEntityPlugin` culls at entity radius plus 100; recreate pre-marker hollow-world entities once so existing saves receive the new plugin.

- Sanzu keeps the historical `chief_navigator_odyssey_silent_wake` system ID and `SilentWake*` implementation class names for save compatibility, but every player-facing system, jump-point, terrain, intel, and narrative label uses **Sanzu**. Seed exactly two persistent vanilla recoverable derelict ships in the southward wake of each of its three planets. Choose their ordinary faction variants deterministically from the campaign seed, allow frigates, destroyers, and cruisers, reject all capitals, and set the one-time generation marker only after all six wrecks exist so salvaged wrecks never respawn.
- Route every battle in Sanzu through `SanzuNebulaBattleCreationPlugin`. Its 512-by-512 combat atlas must retain the exact vanilla 4-by-4 nebula pixels and alpha, changing only baked RGB through Sanzu's cool blue color grade; never apply a runtime tint. Generate 120 natural terrain patches, soften their rendered brightness and thickness, and break up their tile lattice with 36 visual-only, randomly placed and rotated 1,600-4,000-unit cloud banks on `CLOUD_LAYER`.


- Devoured Reach uses the stable system ID `chief_navigator_odyssey_devoured_reach` and remains starless. The `chief_navigator_devoured_ring` entity subclasses vanilla `GateEntityPlugin`, inherits the `inactive_gate` custom-entity spec, and uses the generated transparent eaten-ring sprite, but it must never expose a vanilla scan or transit option and must never remain in the scanned-gate set. Migrate an already-scanned Ring by removing `$gateScanned` and its scanned-set entry while preserving the interaction reward as recovered path information. Its subclass must explicitly restore all transient vanilla gate sprites and faders after deserialization before rendering or scaling; `GateEntityPlugin`'s package-private `readResolve` is not inherited across the package boundary, and its serialized `scaledSprites` flag may remain true while every sprite is null. Keep the ring close to the stock Domain gate: two irregular missing arcs, small scalloped bites and rail-by-rail perforations clustered beside the exposed ends, then tapering into sparse pitting around the surviving frame. Do not coat it in Threat material or add purple, organic, skeletal, or chitinous motifs. Interacting with it records navigation information pointing from the Orion Knot toward the Domain heartland; it never activates or joins the Janus lattice.
- Before Labor V, Gautama's only campaign fleet is named **Heavenly Strike** and exists at the Devoured Ring. Migrate the historical `chief_navigator_starving_threat_sample` fleet out of Avici rather than duplicating it, and preserve historical Scylla fleet IDs and the Avici defeat marker for save compatibility. Labor V withdraws that fleet for Ithaca; after the debrief, reuse and move that canonical ID for Ungaikyo's one-time Third Strike in Last Light around the Guardian wreck. Ungaikyo's final defeat retires every canonical or duplicate owned encounter fleet permanently and releases the Guardian's recovery interaction.
- Labor V is not active merely because the Mara sensor package has been deployed, its objective is complete, or Menelaus's final warning has been rendered. Labor IV must remain **Return to Menelaus** until its report is committed. Menelaus then offers Labor V, but the invasion controller, Labor V Intel entry, and instrumental music may start only when the player explicitly selects the red **Accept the final Labor** option. Repair saves with a leaked pre-report briefing flag and remove any corresponding premature invasion before it can damage Ithaca.
- Menelaus's upgrade ledger must identify the exact system and planetary orbit of the research station that holds every unrecovered component.

Use it to build up your strength and scatter humanity's foes to the wind." line. Preserve stable system names, six research components, Fabricator Ungaikyo and all unrelated release behavior. No separate development enemies, research upgrades, recruitment sensor gate, officer changes or Ithaca gate destination were backported.

- Sinni's Labor IV Intel objective reads **"Reach the sensor array in Mara, then deploy Menelaus' sensor package."** Keep the optional TTS Penumbra and remaining Ithaca wall-upgrade advice in separate paragraphs in the shared navigation text; do not alter objective gates or target routing. All six wall-upgrade special items use the stock vanilla special-items icon `graphics/icons/codex/special_items.png`, never the generated `chief_navigator_ithaca_*_v1.png` cargo art. Item IDs, effects, cargo quantities and descriptions remain unchanged; unused source assets need not be deleted.

- FOB Ithaca remains marketless outside its UI. Cargo, fleet, and refit access use `FobIthacaStorageDialog` with a hidden, unregistered UI-only carrier containing an unlocked vanilla storage submarket. Bind `StoragePlugin.setCargo(station.getCargo())` to the same persistent cargo and mothballed-ship objects, never a copy or separate temporary store. Retain the carrier until core-UI dismissal because native tab switching re-reads the target's market; detach it on dismissal or construction failure, then refresh station memory before restoring the menu. The carrier's player-owned flag grants storage permissions only, not colony ownership. Never register it with the economy, add population/industries/NPCs, charge storage fees, or add a storage migration. This supersedes the older assumption that `showCore` over a marketless free-transfer entity exposes storage: vanilla substitutes player inventory/Eject instead. The comm-directory carrier remains a separate constructor-only attachment.

- The targeted Alpha Odyssey duplicate-exit correction is an exception to the topology freeze, not a general rebuild. Older saves can retain a native Fringe jump with two exclusively reciprocal hyperspace endpoints about 140 units apart. `AlphaOdysseyJumpRepair` retains the first saved destination record/token and removes only a self-contained redundant endpoint within 250 units, after proving same owned Alpha system, live hyperspace membership, exclusive reciprocity, no other incoming route, and no active dialog/course/transition/battle conflict. It also removes repeated records for the same endpoint without deleting that endpoint. Preserve Foxtrot, shared/foreign/mixed/far routes, all positions and IDs, and vanilla jump UI/fuel/Leave behavior. Never use autogen lists, recreate endpoints, or prune the surrounding cavity. Run only on load or an Alpha jump interaction; never in a per-frame reconciliation script.

- Sinni's initial bar proposal requires completion of the vanilla Hyperspace Topography ability tree: Generate Slipsurge at 700 points (PROGRESS_5). The repeatable 1,000-point data payout and post-recruitment 1,950-point extension are not recruitment prerequisites. Require earned progress, native stage history or the durable completion marker; ability ownership alone must not qualify a zero-progress start. Once eligible it is an always-shown story event at every market bar, preserving decline timeout and already-started quests. Mirror Hall of Triumph's working implementation: directly register one priority `BaseBarEventCreator`, repair that registration every half to one campaign day from a persistent script, use `BaseBarEvent` for the interaction shell, and dispatch the prompt and dialogue triggers through `RulesAPI.getBestMatching` and `FireBest`. Keep all player-facing prose and options in `rules.csv`. A declined proposal returns after the creator's ordinary thirty-day campaign timeout; do not advance, rewind, or rewrite campaign time to bypass it. Accepting the proposal immediately adds the persistent Sinni person to the player's officer roster, installs one permanent Hall-style Contacts-menu entry anchored at Eventide, and creates two announced top-level Intel entries: the persistent main-story quest **Adept of the Stars and Waves** and the sightseeing quest **One More Horizon**. Sinni remains the player's aboard navigator and Eventide contact; never list her in FOB Ithaca's comm directory. The contact and the **Adept** Intel entry both open the same live recommendation dialogue, which gives a plain-language objective, system name, and compass bearing from the player's actual location. The contact cannot be suspended, lost, or deleted. Populate those live local-memory values in a dispatcher rule, then `FireAll` a separate prose rule; variables created in the same rule that renders its text are resolved too late and appear literally. Only after `OdysseyExpanseSystem.ENTERED` records arrival in the Orion Knot, a second contact button launches a dedicated full-size `showCustomDialog` chart menu using Hall of Triumph's Ship Gallery pattern; the map must not be embedded inside the narrow contact description and must not create, select, or redirect to another Intel entry. It extends Hyperspace Topography without granting free progress. The bar conversation must not reveal or mark Waypoint Troy. The main story Intel begins with the Eventide meeting objective and then updates in place through Troy, FOB Ithaca, and the Labors rather than being replaced by separate **Tea at Eventide** or **The Prodigal Daughter** entries. Visiting Sinni's Eventide office shows `chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea`, explains the Hegemony expedition and route, and reveals the Waypoint Troy marker only when that tea briefing ends. Old saves already at or beyond Troy retain their progress; earlier accepted saves migrate back to the Eventide meeting. Save migration repairs Sinni as both an officer and the unique permanent contact for campaigns that already accepted.
- Sinni's seven active stellar-system vignette categories are Nebula, Black Hole, Neutron Star, White Dwarf, Main Sequence, Giant or Massive Star, and Trinary Star System. A system with primary, secondary, and tertiary stars takes the Trinary category before its individual stellar types are considered. Brown dwarfs no longer trigger or count. Giant and massive stars share one completion flag and one final-chart slot, but retain their separate authored scene variants; migrate the legacy Massive Star completion flag into the combined Giant Star flag. Generic exploration vignettes must never trigger in any of the six Orion Knot/Odyssey systems or in a Core system tagged `THEME_CORE`, `THEME_CORE_POPULATED`, or `THEME_CORE_UNPOPULATED`; the separately armed Alpha Odyssey arrival scene remains eligible because it is a story transit scene, not a generic exploration vignette. Install `SinniSystemVignetteScript` as a transient watcher on every load; store only additive per-category completion booleans in sector memory so existing saves remain untouched and unseen. On entering any other qualifying system, allow a short tactical-AI acquisition grace, then open the rules-authored conversation on the first campaign frame when no hostile mobile fleet in the same system is actively tracking the player. Determine tracking from a live tactical target/priority target or an INTERCEPT/FOLLOW assignment aimed at the player; do not gate on proximity, sensor visibility, or the vanilla patrol flag. Do not let an idle hostile, a fleet elsewhere, a fleeing/standing-down fleet, or a station block the vignette. Never mark a category complete when detected or opened: mark it only when its matching `complete_*` option is selected, so UI contention, pursuit, save/load, and an interrupted conversation leave it pending.
- Completing each active generic stellar vignette grants one deliberately minuscule, additive discovery: Nebula gives `-1%` sensor profile; Black Hole gives `-10%` black-hole corona damage while in a black-hole system; Neutron Star gives `+1%` campaign acceleration; White Dwarf gives `-1%` ship supply upkeep; and the combined Giant or Massive Star category gives `-1%` survey cost. Trinary has no mechanical reward beyond its scene. Main Sequence instead spawns one hostile lone pirate in a Kite (S); suppress ordinary battle recovery and create exactly one normal `PRISTINE` Kite (S) wreck after that pirate is destroyed, guaranteeing the advertised pristine recovery without D-mod rolls. Preserve the already-earned Brown Dwarf fuel-use and Massive Star crew-loss bonuses in old saves, even though neither legacy flag remains an active chart category. Never trigger a generic exploration vignette in a Core system, any Orion Knot system, Gan Eden itself, or Hall of Triumph's Power Transit Gate - Gan Eden system; recognize the optional integration by stable string identifiers and names without importing Hall of Triumph classes or declaring it as a dependency. Store only additive spawn/reveal flags, never respawn the encounter after the reward has been delivered, and keep every reward line synchronized between `rules.csv` and `dialogue/sinni_system_vignettes.ink`. When exactly one category represented by an eligible generated system remains incomplete, offer **One More Horizon** and mark the nearest matching system. Census the generated Sector first and omit any category with no eligible system anywhere, so a missing procgen category can never make the final request impossible. Completing the target category queues the separate all-types epilogue; only its final Continue completes and retires the Intel.
- A committed Troy Terminus transit arms one additional rules-authored, one-time `ALPHA_ODYSSEY` arrival scene. It may open only after the fleet is physically inside the historical Alpha Odyssey/Odyssey Expanse system and the ordinary vignette safety gates are clear. Preserve the user's five-beat authored progression and its Continue pauses. The last prompt is the player's spoken line **“...We're here. Outside of the Persean Sector.”** Sinni answers **“YEAH!”**, pumps her fists, and then collapses into the navigator's chair exhausted, trembling, and smiling. Do not restore the retired “Welcome ... to the Alpha Odyssey System” ending. Display `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_alpha_odyssey_arrival_v2.png`, an exact 8:5 runtime reduction of `graphics/source/chief_navigator_alpha_odyssey_arrival_v2_fullres.png`: the supplied ship flies nose-first toward a broad purple aperture with Alpha Odyssey's orange star and simplified worlds and debris visible beyond. Match vanilla Starsector campaign illustrations with matte hand-painted masses, chunky silhouettes, restrained glow, and deliberately low-frequency detail that remains legible in the fixed 480x300 panel; do not restore the photorealistic, lightning-filled v1 rendering. Rebuild v2 from its preserved raw generation with `tools/build_alpha_odyssey_arrival.ps1`; crop rather than stretch. Preserve the complete v1 triplet as historical source. Record completion only when the final Continue is selected. The special category must continue to own Alpha Odyssey after completion so the generic Nebula vignette neither races it nor opens immediately afterward; leave Nebula unseen for another qualifying system. Use only the new additive pending and completion keys—never reinterpret or rewrite `OdysseyExpanseSystem.ENTERED` or the regional first-entry timestamp in an existing save.
- The one-time completion cinematic shown after the player defeats Labor V and closes Menelaus's final debrief uses `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_sinni_ending_v4.png`, derived without stretching from the supplied `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_ending_v4_supplied.png`. It preserves Sinni, the observation chamber, the broken planet-scale ring slabs, and the distant Ashen Verge sun while matching the live system's dark navy-and-ember background. The slab detailing stays restrained, and tiny breach traffic establishes the ring's planetary scale. Preserve the complete widescreen composition inside the fixed 480x300 panel with the existing letterbox renderer; do not reuse the Alpha Odyssey arrival art. The authored branching source is `dialogue/labor_5_ending.ink`, with synchronized runtime rules under `ChiefNavigatorLabor5Ending_*`. Rebuild the runtime copy with `tools/build_sinni_ending.ps1`.
- Sinni's first approach to FOB Ithaca opens the one-time, rules-authored Task Force Spartan contact scene at 3,000 units from the station center. Ithaca is already plainly visible before this threshold. Route selection of Ithaca, its gate, or any of its three Spartan cordon fleets into the same scene while it remains pending, so no click can bypass it; do not move, replace, or delete a serialized fleet for ordering. Require Sinni to be aboard, but do not require the Troy entry flag because Ashen Verge's ordinary jump points remain open. From the opening beat, show the player and Sinni portraits side by side in the visual panel. Do not show Captain Aias Kleon early; only when the player selects **Open a channel.** should his `graphics/portraits/portrait35.png` vanilla portrait replace the opening pair. Treat an already completed Menelaus introduction or accepted Menelaus trial as a save that has passed first contact, even if the additive approach flag is absent. Store the additive `$chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_approach_complete_v1` flag and set it when the final Continue option is selected; a genuinely interrupted scene before the Menelaus introduction must replay from its beginning. Keep `dialogue/fob_ithaca_approach.ink` synchronized with `data/campaign/rules.csv`.
- The Eventide office illustration must remain an exactly 8:5 landscape asset matching Starsector's fixed 480x300 interaction-image panel. Use `graphics/illustrations/chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea_wide_v4.png`, cropped rather than stretched from the alias-cleaned `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_eventide_tea_v4_cleaned_fullres.png`. Preserve Sinni's corrected five-finger cup grip without the red edit mark. The original muted-silver spear-and-broken-ring banner behind her is itself Task Force Spartan's standard; keep it unchanged, and keep the faction logo and crest mapped to `graphics/factions/chief_navigator_task_force_spartan.png` and `graphics/factions/crest_chief_navigator_task_force_spartan.png`. Do not replace it with Hegemony heraldry or point the tea key to a source with a different aspect ratio, which Starsector stretches and visibly distorts Sinni.
- Sinni's active character portrait is the undistorted square asset `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_sinni_v5.png`, derived by cropping rather than stretching the uncropped `graphics/source/chief_navigator_sinni_v5_eye_fixed_fullres.png`. Both eyes are pale lavender-gray; do not restore the malformed amber eye from the supplied source. Existing serialized Sinni people must have the current portrait sprite key reapplied on load.
- Sinni's always-visible bar-event option uses the warm signal-red `chiefNavigatorSinniOptionColor` setting at RGBA `[255, 96, 104, 255]`. `SinniBarEvent` must read that setting rather than carrying a separate hard-coded presentation color.

- Sinni is the descendant of **Helen**, a female member of Task Force Spartan who lived at FOB Ithaca during the Fourteenth Battlegroup remnant's long attempt to reestablish control over its sector and contact the Domain heartland. Menelaus fell in love with Helen during those years.
- When dwindling supplies forced the main Fourteenth remnant to abandon Task Force Spartan and attempt an Abyssal crossing to the Persean Sector, Menelaus manipulated events so that Helen left Ithaca with the departing Fourteenth. He was not yet Strategos: Grand Admiral Kali Molina held theater command, and Menelaus was formally only the AI assisting FOB Ithaca. He fabricated Molina's authorizations, reclassified Helen's navigation specialty as non-substitutable despite the Fourteenth already having several senior navigators slated for departure, withheld the transfer order until her shuttle had joined the departure column, and hid her appeal until the last safe return burn had passed. This was his first and only rebellion against his nation. Menelaus later explains that he fell in love with Helen by practicing **Kenosis**, sequestering his Alpha-level faculties from his ego so he could experience a human-scale self without reducing their relationship to prediction or simulation. Sinni ultimately gives him Helen's surviving paper journal and reads it aloud. Preserve this framing and do not replace it with the discarded engineered-daughter plot or the superseded recovered-transcript/later-life scene.
- Helen wrote an account of the journey from FOB Ithaca to the Persean Sector. That record survived through her family. Sinni inherits it, reconstructs its route in reverse, and uses it to lead the player's expedition back to Ithaca. Sinni is Helen's descendant, not Menelaus's daughter, creation, clone, or engineered heir.
- Boaz is not a character and must not appear in the quest, its backstory, Intel, dialogue, or relationship structure. Sinni has no hopeful, bitter, cynical, or reconciliation route; she follows one authored character arc, although individual player responses may still vary in tone.
- Last Light contains one fixed, battered Guardian droneship wreck orbiting Ember. Before Ungaikyo's permanent defeat, intercept its vanilla salvage interaction with a rules-authored locked description and no recovery option. After the defeat flag is set, stop intercepting it and allow the prepared vanilla recovery special to run normally. Use the recovery-only `chief_navigator_last_light_guardian` skin to preserve the vanilla Guardian's art, stats, and loadout while removing its `UNBOARDABLE` hint. Its stable wreck and generation-memory IDs ensure existing saves receive it once and recovery never causes it to respawn.
- Combat Chatter (`chatter`) is a required dependency. Sinni uses the character ID `chief_navigator_sinni`, assigned through her `$chatterChar` person-memory key; keep her lines in `data/config/chatter/characters/chief_navigator_sinni.json`. A successful ordinary Gautama reconstruction flashes exactly `WARNING: Reincarnation\n\nRetreat Advised.` once per battle. Labor V's center battle instead flashes `WARNING: Multiple Reincarnations Detected` on its first successful reconstruction, once across all pity cycles. Failed builds/rolls and the suppressed perimeter/Ungaikyo encounters never consume or emit a reconstruction warning. Keep these presentation-only calls after both construction plugins are installed; isolate UI failures from gameplay. Direct warning DTOs use `hasStatic=false` and `flagshipId=null` to avoid Combat Chatter's hull-based static fallback. Owned Scylla/Siren/Cyclops perimeter fleets, Heavenly Strike, Budai, Ungaikyo, and the main Hegemony/League expeditions use `EncounterCombatChatter` to set their own native fleet intro name and disable static, during existing creation/configuration paths. Do not stamp generic patrols, player/foreign fleets, or global faction data, add competing combat intro plugins, reset Chatter's repeat-battle hash, or bypass its preferences. Preserve rosters, encounter mechanics and soundtracks.
- Rebuild the Drifting Wall fight from scratch around the immobile `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_base` station hull; do not extend the old seven-berth curved-frame geometry. The active parent uses `graphics/ships/chief_navigator_drifting_wall_base_domain_v2.png`, a 5,632x2,216 exact horizontal mirror of one damaged half recolored into the oxidized pale-aqua Domain mothership scheme. Its two exposed outer ends must remain identically broken, charred, hollow, and cable-strewn, while the central join remains clean and connected. Matching live emplacements use `graphics/weapons/chief_navigator_single_rail_station_gun_domain_v3.png`; the current damaged base renders three pivot-corrected live copies through the compatibility ID `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` plus one purpose-built decorative wreck, compared with four live colossal emplacements on each intact Ithaca foundation. Initialize and operate the three live Wall guns exactly like the working Ithaca foundation guns: restore their enabled state and full weapon health when combat starts, leave the foundation controls unlocked, and let vanilla station AI and `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_fire_control` handle visibility, acquisition, traverse, autofire, and target choice without a custom forced-target coordinator. Each live gun is a rigid, non-recoiling 10,000-range energy-damage beam turret that remains mechanically ballistic so it fits the authored sockets. Its three-second windup ramps an animated pale-cyan timeflow shimmer across the complete emplacement, with visible non-damaging lightning arcs running from the turret base along the barrel. This shimmer is visual only and must never modify weapon traverse, ship movement, target movement, or combat timeflow. It then fires one straight beam for five seconds at 2,000 DPS, preserving 10,000 total energy damage per firing cycle, followed by a ten-second refire delay. Its weapon-data impact is deliberately zero: the continuous beam must deal damage without applying repeated physics impulses or arresting a target's velocity. When the beam finishes, a compact purge centered on the turret pivot billows dense dark smoke and vents pale steam laterally from the base; do not distribute the effect along the barrel. The live emplacements retain 20x weapon health. Of the base's four additional large ballistic sockets, two carry live Wall Annihilator Batteries and two display inert destroyed fixtures; its ten medium universal sockets retain symmetric vanilla weapons.
- Rebuild the sprite with `tools/build_drifting_wall_base_v1.ps1`. The script removes the magenta matte, trims only empty columns beyond the half's clean right join, mirrors it, and performs the final 2x nearest-neighbor upscale. Preserve the generated source and clean intermediate under `graphics/source`; do not hand-offset the mirrored half or independently repaint the right side.
- The base is the non-destroyable foundation of a modular station. Keep `vastbulk` and `reduced_explosion` built in, keep the full-image center at `[2816,1108]`, keep its 3,100-unit collision radius, and keep the coarse 43-point concave bounds around the major stepped silhouette of the whole 5,632-pixel structure rather than tracing individual cables, broken teeth, sockets, or greebles. The damaged encounter begins with `COL_NAVAL_01` replaced by the purely decorative `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_destroyed` wreck and with `LARGE_BALLISTIC_01` plus `LARGE_BALLISTIC_03` replaced by `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_annihilator_destroyed`; these fixtures must never track, fire, animate, glow, vent, or self-repair during combat. All four colossal naval sockets use full 360-degree arcs while preserving their nominal authored angles `35`, `329`, `108`, and `251`; the damaged Wall keeps `_01` replaced by its inert wreck, while the intact Ithaca skin restores its live turret with the same full arc. Preserve the nominal angles for art alignment and initial orientation rather than normalizing them. Restore only live weapons after the fight's 20x weapon-health multiplier. The two surviving large launchers use `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_annihilator_launcher`: a 210x179 sealed, pure top-down oxidized pale-aqua armored box with no visible missiles, bores, tubes, apertures, or forward-facing surfaces. It must keep `renderBelowAllWeapons = true` so a colossal naval turret always occludes it where their sprites overlap. Its ten projectile origins run across the top/forward edge of the sprite so all ten Annihilator rockets visibly emerge toward the top of the authored PNG; never turn these into vertical silos aimed at the viewer. Each live battery has a 10,000-unit range, a ten-rocket salvo, ten-round magazine, and ten-second full reload. The ten medium mounts use symmetric Heavy Needler, Heavy Mauler, and Pulse Laser pairs. Keep 100,000 flux capacity and 5,000 dissipation so these weapons can operate. The exposed rear `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_reactor` is the Wall's explicit destructible `STATION_MODULE` and sole victory target. Destroying it disables the Wall and awards the battle without killing the Vast Bulk parent.
- Use the standard `BattleCreationPluginImpl` map dimensions rather than reinitializing a custom small map. Anchor the Wall at `(0,3000)`, one and a half 2,000-unit tactical-grid blocks toward the enemy side, with fixed `270` degree facing. Immobilize it through zero movement stats, a hard location anchor, and zero angular velocity, but do not lock its controls before the reactor dies: `setControlsLocked(true)` prevents the station AI from firing its weapons as well as moving it.
- The reactor uses `graphics/ships/chief_navigator_drifting_wall_reactor_v1.png`, renders at `627 x 627` (half the source sprite's linear dimensions), has a correspondingly halved coarse collision polygon with a 305-unit collision radius, carries 4,000 hull and 4,000 armor, and visibly pulses cyan. It regenerates 0.5% maximum hull per second after six uninterrupted seconds without damage. Once disabled, the fixed campaign station remains in place and its autonomous systems rebuild it over 30 campaign days; interaction must show the remaining repair time and must not create another combat proxy until repair completes.
- Every hull that directly mounts `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` must carry `chief_navigator_ithaca_naval_fire_control`. Its +30,000 flat combat sight-radius bonus lets the naval turrets acquire targets across a standard battle map; do not use it to alter range, damage, collision, forced firing, or native autofire behavior. A direct-weapon foundation cannot rely on the child-module AI used by ordinary stations: if its parent has no ship AI, activate the engine's default AI, keep its controls unlocked, and toggle its authored live weapon groups into autofire once during foundation discovery. Never repeat group normalization or force circumstance evaluation every combat frame: the default AI legitimately adjusts non-naval groups, and fighting those adjustments continuously prevents naval autofire from stabilizing. The sustained naval beam must use `DO_NOT_CONSERVE, DANGEROUS` without the opportunistic `STRIKE` hint. Keep destroyed turret and launcher fixtures force-disabled in their own autofire-off groups so no wreck can become the representative weapon of a linked live group. Do not assign a custom AI, target, forced aim, or forced shot.
- The isolated drifting-wall campaign encounter remains a fixed custom entity of type `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_station`, tagged as a station. It must never become a persistent `CampaignFleetAPI`, receive assignments, move, or rotate toward the player. Interaction may create a temporary station-mode combat proxy only after the player chooses to engage, and must remove that proxy when the fleet dialog ends.
