- Existing mega-wall weapon art remains available for later child-module design, but none of its old seven-slot placement, rotation, seating correction, or legacy frame/insert darkness rules constrain the rebuilt base.
- The Odyssey prototype reserves one six-system Alpha Odyssey clearing in remote Abyssal hyperspace southeast of and beyond the ordinary campaign-map rectangle. With vanilla dimensions its center is approximately `(+150,000, -94,000)`, so an expanded map shows the ordinary Sector in the upper-left and Alpha Odyssey alone in the lower-right. The clearing is a compact true circular pocket of ordinary hyperspace: its inner 7,400 world-unit radius has zero Abyssal depth, followed by a smooth 2,200-unit transition back into the untouched surrounding Abyss. Rebuild the old 9,600-20,000 annulus from nearby untouched Abyss tiles when migrating older saves so the pocket visibly tightens instead of retaining its previous oversized clearing. Both the terrain tiles and the global Abyss plugin must respect this pocket so its background, mechanics, and contained system classification are normal rather than merely an empty black patch. The principal route runs northwest-to-southeast and strictly descends on the map: the Odyssey Expanse/Alpha Odyssey entry sits at relative `(2000, 1000)`, Sanzu is exactly between it and Avici at `(4250, -2250)`, and Avici lies farther south on the map at `(6500, -5500)`. Each leg is about 3,950 units. Ashen Verge lies east of Alpha Odyssey at `(5900, 1000)`, Last Light lies south and slightly west at `(800, -2700)`, and Devoured Reach lies north of Ashen Verge at `(5250, 4845)`; their neighboring legs use the same approximate spacing. Keep those six system centers fixed, but compress each system's own hyperspace footprint so its entrances and body wells read as one local group instead of blending into neighboring systems: ordinary authored entrances use stable irregular offsets of roughly 380-800 units, Avici's inward-facing fan uses roughly 650-1,100 units, ordinary system hyperspace radii are 1,100, Avici is 1,200, and the larger Alpha Odyssey nebula is 1,450. The cluster is not hidden content: remove `THEME_HIDDEN`, keep its stars visible on the map, and expose sixteen authored, clickable hyperspace `JumpPointAPI` entities—three paired with each of five systems and one paired with Devoured Reach. Only Foxtrot Terminus Aperture inside Alpha Odyssey, reached through Sinni's Waypoint Troy sequence, is story-gated; the sixteen system jump points are ordinary navigation points. All sixteen system jump points are presented as normal open navigation points. Give every hyperspace and in-system jump point planet-style permanent visibility: clear its sensor profile, discoverability override, finite detected-range modifier, and extended-range fade so it remains named and marked on the map regardless of distance. In addition to those ordinary passages, create and reconcile one permanently exposed vanilla `NascentGravityWellAPI` for every authored celestial body in every system: the primary sun or black hole, every ordinary planet, and all three custom hollow worlds in starless Devoured Reach each receive their own body-targeted well, matching vanilla's visible celestial wells. Rebuild the former single-primary well scheme once in older saves and remove only duplicate or stale wells belonging to the same system. Avici's primary hyperspace jump point retains its 350-unit interaction radius but uses the same normal open jump-point visual and behavior as the other fifteen points. Aggressively remove every other procgen or untracked jump across the previous 20,000-unit cavity footprint while preserving Foxtrot Terminus Aperture as the Alpha Odyssey endpoint from Waypoint Troy, all sixteen authored cluster jump points, and every reconciled body well. Do not restore the shared destination portal or red/violet boundary-spill renderer. The normally generated Odyssey Expanse nebula contains Foxtrot Terminus Aperture as the Troy-route arrival endpoint, along with the drifting wall and Task Force Spartan. Avici is the neighboring black-hole system; it contains no THREAT or rogue-derelict fleets, and Budai is its only roaming hostile fleet. Its black fog must leave a moving zero-density clearing within 1,250 units of Kshaya Habitat, feathering back in by 2,250 units, so neither the rendered fog nor its Abyssal campaign penalties can cover the habitat or its approach as the station orbits. Devoured Reach is the starless sixth system containing the Devoured Ring, three hollow planet entities, twenty-four renewable feeding masses, and Gautama's unique Heavenly Strike fleet. Budai functions as an Avici-bound vengeance hunter: he tracks and pursues the player only inside Avici, returns to his Kshaya-area lair when they leave, and never enters hyperspace or another system. Every ordinary in-system jump point leads only to its paired hyperspace entrance; no ordinary point directly links two realspace systems. Sanzu is a fixed top-to-bottom nebula river whose streamlines combine multi-cylinder potential flow with a deterministic von Karman vortex street: six alternating counter-rotating eddies shed behind each star and planet, widen slightly, and decay downstream. Keep this field precomputed; do not replace it with frame-by-frame fluid simulation. Each foreground and background smoke clump has a deterministic 30% chance to follow a second precomputed route set that ignores the three planets while still diverting around the white dwarf, allowing some fog to wash over planetary faces instead of every clump bouncing around the limbs. Avici has the starfield-backed, opaque pitch-black nebula banks; rendering and terrain effects use the same deterministic mask, with full-density clouds applying vanilla Abyssal hyperspace burn, visibility, and sensor multipliers. Ashen Verge is clear normal space containing FOB Ithaca and must not retain the old black-cloud anchor. The serialized cloud type keeps its historical `chief_navigator_ashen_verge_clouds` ID solely so older saves load before migration. Last Light is a sparse white-dwarf system whose standalone Time Omega boss is unrelated to Sinni's story. Do not restore procgen scatter or the pure-black fake-sector map, hallway locations, Veiled Sun room link, automatic map opening, or Transverse Jump lock.
- Superseding the older Last Light sentence in the preceding cluster-layout note: Last Light has no active Time Omega boss. It contains the Guardian wreck and receives Ungaikyo's one-time Third Strike after Labor V; the Guardian's recovery interaction unlocks only after Ungaikyo is permanently defeated.
- Alpha Odyssey exploration loops **Rising Up** by Ryini Beats through the system's native `$musicSetId`, yields to combat music, and resumes through Starsector's ordinary location-music state. Do not restore a one-shot campaign script: replacement `MusicPlayerPlugin` implementations such as Music Switcher honor the native system key but can discard imperative requests. Always retain explicit Ryini Beats production credit, the source URL, and the artist's stated noncommercial-use condition in both the README and audio-source record when distributing this asset.
- FOB Ithaca is located inside Ashen Verge, whose primary is a brown dwarf rather than a white dwarf. Its campaign representation is one fixed, named, clickable station entity at `(4500, 0)`, rendered on `STATIONS` with the complete transparent sprite `graphics/campaign/fob_ithaca/ithaca_station.png` at `1500 x 1500` world units and the live outer `ithaca_orbital_ring_v4.png` at `3800 x 3800`. Preserve the v4 ring's three intact cardinal bays, each using the same wide stepped fortress silhouette and the Drifting Wall's oxidized pale-aqua/teal Domain armor; keep the connecting rails cold gray, gunmetal, and charcoal with restrained aged-copper seams. Remove all antennae, dishes, masts, and booms. The entire left bay must remain missing, along with a broad adjoining arc of both ring rails, so the damage reads as one large physical breach between two jagged destroyed stumps rather than a damaged module. Multiply both structural sprites by the shared opaque `(115, 110, 115)` tint so they sit at roughly 45% source brightness against Ashen Verge; do not bake this darkness destructively into the PNGs. The aligned amber equipment-light masks remain available as unused source assets, but both additive glow alphas must stay at `0`, so neither the ring nor central FOB renders a glow. Do not add enlarged or diffuse glow passes. The former eight-piece procedural ring and original/v2/v3 orbital hoops remain only as unused source art and must not be assembled or rendered at runtime. Visual sizes, fixed angles, structural tint, glow alpha, twinkle, image-centering offsets, and the `100000`-unit always-visible render range are controlled at the top of `FobIthacaCampaignEntityPlugin`; keep `docs/FOB_ITHACA_PLACEMENT.md` current whenever those controls change. The custom-entity spec must likewise advertise the full `3800 x 3800` visual footprint instead of the transparent controller texture's native one-pixel dimensions. Treat Ithaca like a planet rather than a sensor contact: on every load, clear its sensor profile and discoverable override to `null`, remove stale detected-range overrides, and let the known landmark render continuously; do not try to simulate this with enormous sensor ranges or forced per-frame fader brightness. The Drifting Wall campaign renderer uses the same `100000`-unit range. These render ranges are deliberately independent of interaction and collision radii so both large structures are rendered whenever any portion is on screen. The installation must never rotate to face the player. On every load, convert the Ashen Verge primary to `StarTypes.BROWN_DWARF`, remove any stale Ithaca entity from another system, and create or repair the Ashen Verge entity; keep its important marker and map label so it is plainly visible from the arrival area rather than hidden scenery. Preserve the existing custom entity type, persistent ID, and plugin class so saves migrate in place. Interaction with this campaign entity selects one of the three intact bastion encounters.
- The north, east, and south FOB Ithaca bastions are three persistent station fights built on the Drifting Wall's Vast Bulk architecture. Each uses `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_base_Standard` with the exact full teal station artwork in `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_generated_v1.png`; rebuild its 5,632x2,216 runtime canvas only with `tools/build_fob_ithaca_generated_base.ps1`. Preserve the complete generated geometry and keyed alpha in `graphics/source/chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_generated_v1_keyed.png`; do not reconstruct it from halves. The older mirrored-half asset remains unused alternate source art. All four colossal naval guns, all four Wall Annihilator Batteries, and all ten medium emplacements begin live. The exposed self-repairing rear reactor is the sole victory target. Use the standard Drifting Wall battle-map setup, hard-anchor the selected foundation at `(0,3000)` with `270`-degree facing, and keep its controls unlocked so station AI can fire. Nearby intact campaign stations may reinforce at 6,000-unit horizontal offsets. Each participating bastion receives a five-drone Domain Combat Guard screen spawned by the battle plugin in a safe formation ahead of its foundation; never store mobile escorts inside a station-mode campaign fleet, because vanilla station deployment places them at the foundation origin and destroys them through collision overlap. Do not restore the retired curved wall-segment, seven berth modules, rear bulkhead, separate Ithaca core, or custom oversized wall map.
- Labor V is a four-wave Ithaca defense. Invoke the same three-Third-Strike construction path used by `SpawnSTInvasionFleets`, but while Labor V is active place each fleet at a safe siege token outside its assigned north, east, or south bastion instead of starting an autoresolved station battle. Give every lane a persistent 60-day deadline. If its fleet is still alive then, permanently disable that bastion and send the survivor against its assigned TFS formation. Record a lane victory only when that invasion fleet is actually destroyed, by the player or TFS, and never replace it. Do not place Gautama in those fleets: scrub `chief_navigator_scylla` from serialized perimeter rosters before battle. Loop the instrumental `chief_navigator_final_labor_mission` cue from the moment the three fleets materialize through all three invasion battles and the approach to the gate; use state-aware campaign/combat keepers so transition-time audio requests are retried without restarting a track that is already playing. Maintain the visible, fightable `Gate Defense Station` campaign fleet at `(-1620,0)` relative to Ithaca, centered in the western ring gap, and re-pin it every frame. Only after all three invasion fleets are dead may `chief_navigator_final_labor_heavenly_strike` spawn due west of this station. Give Gautama thirty-three supporting hulls divided into three exact mixed combat waves; every wave contains one Fabricator, one Hive, and nine assault, line, overseer, and skirmish escorts. Hold the later two waves out of the initial combat roster, then deploy their original campaign members together as each preceding force thins, with a bounded timing fallback and a fail-open return to vanilla reserves if staging is incompatible. The final fleet must attack the real Gate Defense Station through the Nex-style invisible-token `ATTACK_LOCATION` -> `GO_TO_LOCATION` -> real-fleet `INTERCEPT` queue and keep the station as its tactical priority. Teleport every surviving, attrition-persistent Task Force Spartan formation into a defensive arc west of the station exactly once and assign them to defend it. The final battle is the only code path allowed to start the looping vocal `chief_navigator_final_labor_fight` cue; the persistent Gate Defense Station joins it and the plugin spawns two additional hard-anchored low-tech reserve battlestations using the legacy-named `chief_navigator_spartan_battlestation_Teal` variant. Apply the Ithaca Wall's material-selective, luminance-preserving oxidized-aqua recolor to the parent and every attached module instead of a whole-sprite runtime tint; preserve the parent sprite's circular central foundation and render `chief_navigator_drifting_wall_colossal_inert` from a centered parent-hull slot above that foundation. The battlestation hull and its custom module skins cost zero deployment points. Do not install the 960-pixel Ithaca naval-mount child at the station center. Never store the two reserve battlestations in a campaign fleet. All sides deal ordinary unmodified combat damage; the 60-day perimeter pacing exists only in the campaign siege timer. Suppress Gautama reconstruction in the perimeter battles. In the center battle, allow repeated reincarnation after his death using each destroyed eligible hull's normal size-based roll plus ten percentage points for every prior failed roll, capped at certainty and reset after a successful construction. A player defeat or retreat that leaves the Heavenly Strike victorious permanently fails Labor V and starts one renewable First- or Second-Strike Gate Incursion at every gate in the Sector, including Core World gates; defeated incursions return after a stable 18-32 day delay. `ForceFinalLabor` is the test-only bypass: it stores a dedicated prerequisite override instead of completing or deleting earlier Labor content, refuses to reset fleets that are in battle, restores the three bastions and full opening TFS rosters, then rebuilds Labor V's opening state and refreshes its Intel immediately.
- Domain Combat Guard is the hidden autonomous Domain Explorarium subfaction at Ithaca, registered as `chief_navigator_domain_combat_guard`. Authored mobile Derelict populations use the complete teal Combat Guard skin set: Warden, Defender, Picket, Sentry, Rampart, Bastillon, and Berserker; the Guardian skin is reserved for authored Guardian uses. The recolored skins preserve vanilla Derelict geometry pixel-for-pixel while narrowly shifting green armor into a muted, low-saturation version of Ithaca's oxidized pale-aqua/teal range; yellow markings, rust, exposed machinery, lighting, and alpha remain unchanged. Rebuild the source-derived PNGs only with `tools/recolor_domain_combat_guard.ps1`; never overwrite the vanilla core assets. FOB Ithaca's center and three campaign bastions recognize the player's IFF and reinforce the player in nearby battles. The Guard is cooperative with the player and hostile to unrelated ordinary Derelicts; reassert these relations when loading older saves.
- Menelaus calls his progression **Labors**, never quests or a trial. His opening line is: **"Rejoice. Hercules had to do twelve labors. I will give you five, one at a time."** Use five separate sequential Intel entries: destroy the four Guard Motherships (`0/4`), deliver and install any four distinct Ithaca defense components (`0/4`) while all six research stations and components remain individually tracked and recoverable and the final two remain optional, regain the Drifting Wall (`0/1`), deploy a black-box sensor package at the Mara Observation Array in Devoured Reach without an aware hostile fleet nearby (`0/1`), then defeat Gautama (`0/1`). The sensor package is mission state, never cargo. Labor IV also reveals a separate optional objective for the normally salvageable TTS Penumbra in Sanzu; Sinni mentions the optional hunt before recovery and recommends the recovered phase ship for the Mara survey-communications deployment afterward without replacing Mara as the primary objective. Every Labor has a separate field-objective-complete state followed by an explicit return to FOB Ithaca and report to Menelaus; keep the current Intel alive, rename it to **Return to Menelaus**, point it at Ithaca, and open the next Labor only when that report is committed. Migrate older saves by preserving the stage they had already reached and treating only prior stages as reported. The Wall remains challengeable before Labor III; an early victory is acknowledged by Menelaus but still requires its normal Labor III report once the sequence reaches it. Accepting Labor I uploads and marks all four motherships and grants the Domain-Security IFF Transponder. Mark only the current Labor's unfinished field targets; the optional Penumbra marker may remain until recovery. Each hostile mothership stays fixed at its authored location as an exact vanilla `derelict_mothership` entity. Interacting with one creates a temporary battle fleet led by the exact vanilla modular `station_derelict_survey_mothership_Standard`, so the player fights and destroys the mothership itself as in the base game. Its escort remains exactly six teal Domain Combat Guard frigates: two Wardens, two Defenders, one Picket, and one Sentry. Never add a Guardian, Rampart, Bastillon, Berserker, cruiser, or any other capital hull to a mothership defense. The vanilla mothership remains unrecoverable through its station hull rules, but never apply a fleet-wide no-recovery flag: disabled Combat Guard escorts must enter ordinary post-battle recovery. The IFF never changes recovery eligibility. After an automated hull tagged `derelict` is recovered through normal encounter rules, it receives a permanent built-in `chief_navigator_domain_security_iff` hullmod and the vanilla no-automated-penalty variant tag, allowing command without Automated Ships expertise. Remnant vessels remain unaffected. Disabling the Wall's reactor permanently gives it player IFF. Across the first three Labors, recovered records point toward the Devoured Ring. Labor IV sends the player into Devoured Reach to deploy the sensor package and confirm that the Threat have spent centuries consuming the ring while trying, unsuccessfully, to reverse-engineer a Janus Device; this is why Menelaus refuses to let the player scan the gate housed inside FOB Ithaca. Reporting Labor IV opens Labor V: Gautama coordinates three perimeter Third Strikes, each with a 60-day siege clock against its own Ithaca bastion. A surviving Strike destroys that bastion at its deadline, then attacks an assigned TFS formation; TFS casualties remain lost for the rest of the Labor. The Heavenly Strike cannot materialize until all three perimeter fleets are destroyed, at which point every surviving TFS formation teleports into the center engagement. Defeating Gautama ends the siege field objective; reporting the victory completes all Labors. Reactivating and scanning the Devoured Ring unknowingly supplies the missing reciprocal topology and connects the Threat feeding ground to the Core Worlds gate lattice. Only full completion grows Ashen Verge's same six allied patrol IDs from three hulls into nine-hull armadas. Menelaus is a persistent alpha-core person displayed at Ithaca with `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_menelaus.png`. His portrait follows the vanilla AI-portrait language: a blue/cyan Corinthian helmet and Greek plume emerge only partially from a dense, unstable signal field. A broken luminous halo sits behind the helmet, while stochastic rays of varied length, spacing, intensity, and fragmentation radiate outward from his eyes like a rising sun. A restrained magenta/violet registration bloom may fringe the white-blue eyes, selected halo arcs, and sparse signal fragments, but blue/cyan remains dominant. Avoid dominant orthogonal grids, clean helmet illustration, perfect radial regularity, or physical metal.
- Defeated Guard Mothership POIs remain absent through the Labor I field phase. Committing the Labor I report causes all four to reconstruct at their exact authored coordinates as friendly Domain Combat Guard motherships. Each restored mothership maintains exactly two persistent three-ship Guard patrols, for eight total. Missing patrols respawn beside their parent mothership, travel to the Drifting Wall, and take `ORBIT_AGGRESSIVE` assignments there. Their ordinary patrol ring remains wall-centered; a fleet more than `2400` units from the Wall is returned in place to one of eight deterministic points on a `950`-unit ring, with its velocity cleared and without replacing its serialized roster. Never apply this correction while the fleet is in battle, hyperspace transition, despawning, player-controlled, or the target of an open interaction dialog. The Wall interaction must show the ordinary pre-battle fleet preview, preserve `pullInAllies = true`, and use a temporary hostile Derelict-faction combat proxy rather than a Domain Combat Guard proxy so every nearby restored patrol can appear in the preview and assist on the player's side. Keep the ten unmoored teal Guard packs in the Odyssey Expanse as vanilla-Derelict-faction hostiles. The friendly Domain Combat Guard faction and the unmoored Derelict faction must remain mutually hostile, while both populations continue to permit ordinary recovery of disabled Guard hulls.
- The Drifting Wall may be challenged whenever it remains hostile and is not rebuilding, including before the player accepts or reaches Labor III; this supersedes the older Labor-III-only assault restriction above. An early victory permanently reclaims the Wall, records Labor III as automatically complete when the preceding Labors finish, and triggers a one-time surprised reaction from Menelaus at the next Ithaca conversation before returning to the current Labor report.
- The authoritative Menelaus portrait is the exact user-supplied 124x124 RGBA image installed at `graphics/portraits/chief_navigator_menelaus.png` (SHA-256 `C661C39563CB402FEA6F2496BB553AA92B7709A4BE4B00B51C349372CEB936B9`). Do not regenerate, recolor, sharpen, pad, or rescale it; this exact asset supersedes the earlier generative portrait-design guidance above.
- FOB Ithaca's surviving outer-ring metal is physically impassable on the campaign layer, while its interior remains navigable. Approximate the visible 320-degree structure with 29 overlapping invisible 185-unit collision entities along a 1,620-unit centerline radius; leave the damaged 40-degree western gap as the sole entrance. Because custom campaign-entity radii are not intrinsically solid, `FobIthacaRingCollisionScript` must also resolve every mobile fleet against that same annular arc every frame, canceling only its inward wall-crossing velocity and preserving tangential motion. In the same frame, re-pin each backing bastion fleet to its authored 2,150-unit fixed position and zero its campaign velocity: station mode and disabled AI do not prevent hostile fleet collision from translating a campaign station. Do not replace this with one center-sized collision circle, which would make Menelaus and the interior gate unreachable. The sole central interaction target is the 750-unit-radius station body. North, east, and south are persistent `CampaignFleetAPI` stations with stable IDs, station mode, independent combat fleets, and fixed positions beneath their matching baked ring modules. Keep these three station entities non-clickable and suppress only their idle flagship campaign sprites so the composite ring art remains the single visual representation; do not revert them to temporary combat proxies. The center selects which station is the objective, while the other active stations retain normal station reinforcement eligibility. A multi-station battle tiles every participating 8,000-unit emplacement and rear bulkhead into one continuous defensive line, keeps the selected station central, and persists every core destroyed during the engagement.
- FOB Ithaca contains a real vanilla inactive-gate entity with the stable id `chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_gate`, fixed in the western interior of the surviving ring. `TreadmillCampaignPlugin` must always intercept that exact entity and route it to the `ChiefNavigatorIthacaGate` rules dialog, which exposes no scan action. A persistent visible Hegemony picket named **Task Force Spartan Gate Watch** orbits the gate under a non-hostile sealed-cordon assignment; it is narrative enforcement of Menelaus's prohibition, not a second gate-combat encounter. Never allow the inactive gate's vanilla scanning dialog to become reachable at any Labor stage.
- FOB Ithaca's center uses a marketless custom station interaction that recreates colony-standard fleet, cargo, and refit access over free-transfer entity cargo, while its contact option opens Starsector's native Comm Directory through a short-lived, non-economy carrier market. Vanilla's directory constructor reads the interaction target's market despite accepting a directory argument, so attach the carrier only around the synchronous `showCommDirectoryDialog()` call and restore the prior market in `finally`; never register it with the economy or leave it attached. The station plugin must implement `RuleBasedDialog` so native person selection can run `OpenCDE` and the rules-authored `PickGreeting` flow. Never replace the native directory with a custom portrait overlay. Menelaus owns the Labors conversation through his directory entry rather than the station entity's root interaction. The north, east, and south ring modules are separate persistent `CampaignFleetAPI` stations. Each campaign fleet contains only one intact Vast Bulk foundation, is fixed at its matching campaign-art module, is directly clickable and attackable, and carries `$chief_navigator_ithaca_section_combat` so it routes into `IthacaSectionBattleCreationPlugin` instead of vanilla station combat. Its five-ship Domain Combat Guard screen is added at safe explicit combat coordinates by that plugin, not stored in the station fleet. Keep idle flagship sprites suppressed behind the baked orbital-ring art, but restore the complete 5,632x2,216 intact sprite before interaction so pulled-in station reinforcements render correctly. The encounter loader must replace older saves' `chief_navigator_ithaca_wall_segment` members with `chief_navigator_ithaca_intact_base_Standard`. Do not restore the retired center-menu assault dispatch, curved wall sprite override, rear bulkheads, or separate control cores.
- Sanzu uses exactly 57 baked flow paths. Its animated clouds are code-rendered overlapping soft elliptical gradients and use no cloud sprite or atlas. Do not use clouds from `nebula_amber`, `nebula512`, `_blue`, `nebula`, `nebula_colorless`, `fx_clouds00`, or `cleaner_clouds00`, and never render a whole cloud atlas as one sprite.
- Sanzu adds a constant southward current of 30 world units per campaign advance second, equally while idle and while steering. Apply it in SilentWakeCurrentTerrain with the public fleet.setLocation setter, which synchronizes the entity and its movement module; directly editing getLocation is overwritten. Preserve propulsion velocity and active navigation targets. Carry only an already-reached idle hold point along with the fleet; a changed destination or fresh mouse input releases that hold point. Never use isFollowingDirectCommand as proof of movement: live traces show it remains true after arrival. Keep the shared white-dwarf/planet lee geometry, transition and pause guards, and idempotent save migration. Do not reintroduce idle-versus-steering force strengths or reflection.
- Sanzu's 57 baked paths guide cloud motion and sparse short directional glints; they are not separately filled visible ribbons. Do not render the former two-layer slipstream-textured mesh: its repeating longitudinal UVs create distracting horizontal stripes, and the foreground/background cloud banks now provide the continuous visual body of the current. Do not restore the older parallel solid-strip renderer either.
- Retain the analytical multi-cylinder velocity-potential helper for possible non-repeating phase effects, but do not feed repeating slipstream textures through it. The current visual is carried by cloud particles moving through the deterministic vortex-street path geometry.
- Sanzu's procedural mist must not form a regular dot or leopard-print lattice. Populate every flow path with 40 clumps whose hashed positions are not constrained to equal longitudinal slots. Use a deliberately broad, nonlinear base-size distribution of roughly 360-1,000 world units, with width never below the base size and height never below 78% of it; the system mist's minimum feature scale must not fall below the visible planet-fog feature scale. Independently vary lateral displacement, aspect, rotation, opacity, and three to seven irregular satellite lobes so the wider stream naturally produces both substantial wisps and occasional broad banks. Include a broad faint skirt around each clump so nearby smoke merges while remaining deterministic across saves.
- Sanzu also has a distinct background-depth cloud pass on `TERRAIN_1`, beneath the sparse flow highlights and the brighter main mist on `TERRAIN_2`. The custom-entity spec must register both terrain layers or the deep pass is never dispatched. It uses 20 darker, broader clumps per path, a 520-1,500 world-unit base-size range, wider lateral displacement, clearly legible blue-black opacity, and 58% of the foreground drift rate. Keep it visually subordinate without reducing it to an imperceptible same-layer underpainting.
- Sanzu's two system-scale cloud layers use independent motion phases so they can move at half the original cloud speed without slowing the textured current, sparse direction glints, planet shader, or fallback planet wisps. The brighter system mist advances at `0.006` phase units per campaign-time unit; the deep background pass retains 58% of that speed (`0.00348`).
- Sanzu's three planets have a separate GLSL 1.20 foreground-vapor pass above their sprites. It uses layered FBM density, slow curl-noise UV distortion, an analytic circular face mask, a noise-eroded elliptical upstream cap/limb, three noise-warped lower tendrils, and a soft radial integration collar to resemble dry ice flowing over a rounded vessel. The foreground pass uses one generously padded quad whose edges lie beyond every nonzero analytic mask; do not split it into overlapping circular and wake meshes, since their entrance and overlap boundaries produce visible straight and curved lines. Do not restore the separable rectangular cap mask, the minimum-density haze term, or any rectangular boundary multiplier: all visible alpha must come from FBM density or filaments, leaving the padded quad completely transparent outside the organic masks. The lower tendrils begin a noise-perturbed opacity falloff at roughly 2.7 planet radii downstream and square that falloff before becoming fully transparent near 5 radii; this long uneven dissolution prevents the trail from reading as a distinct attached object. Its colors must remain in the surrounding stream's blue palette rather than approaching white; its fine feature scale is the visual reference for the wider stream, not something to enlarge to match the old oversized clumps. The `chief_navigator_silent_wake_flow` custom-entity spec must stay registered on both `TERRAIN_2` and `ABOVE_STATIONS`; otherwise Starsector never dispatches the foreground shader branch. Preserve the procedural ellipse implementation only as the automatic shader-failure fallback; the star does not receive either foreground pass.
- A GraphicsLib `RippleDistortion` is automatically removed as soon as its animation frame reaches `60`, regardless of a long configured lifetime. Charybdis's maintained field must use `frameRate = 0`, a fixed safe `currentFrame` below `60`, fixed intensity, and a 1,000-unit base radius that becomes 4,000 units after its one-time 50%-hull enrage. The prototype uses the complete stock Shrouded Maw renderer, including its WobblyParts and smoke, scaled to 150% and blue-shifted by adding 36% of each source color's red channel to its blue channel through a custom Maw creator. Do not clear the plugin parts or force the hidden underlying hull sprite visible.
- Charybdis has 1.4x base hull and 0.5x base armor, and the distortion supplies its encounter rules. Its built-in Pseudoparticle Manifold replaces only its two stock jets with dedicated variants that are 4x as wide and deal 2.5x damage. Its six dedicated Hungering Rift launchers fire missiles with 2x visual/collision dimensions and 1.5x missile hitpoints. The first time it reaches 50% hull, play the original harsh, inharmonic Budai screech and force four rapid Convulsive Lunges in random directions over independently random distances. Only after the fourth lunge, expand the current distortion radius to 4x base, overlay a restrained red tint within that circle, and halve only those Rift launchers' refire delay; do not double its other weapons. Projectile spawn points and live beam origins outside the current radius deal 50% damage to Charybdis and raise a rate-limited `DEFLECTED!` impact message, except that high-explosive hits against its armor or hull always deal normal full damage because their detonation is treated as expanding from inside the bubble. Every ship inside receives a 50% modifier to speed, acceleration, deceleration, turn speed, and turn acceleration; hostile ships are also struck by periodic Shrouded Lens arcs. Do not replace this with the fallback global outgoing-damage buff unless the direct shot-origin listener stops functioning against a future Starsector API.
- Budai retains the stock Maw's offensive Convulsive Lunge behavior through its dedicated variable-distance system and has no combat healing or regeneration mechanic. It never retreats or receives the retired low-hull cooldown penalty. While a hostile non-fighter is close and in front, its dedicated system AI adds a 900-unit backward lunge about every six unpaused combat seconds, respecting normal activation, flux, cooldown, and map-edge constraints. These backward lunges yield to the four random lunges in the one-shot 50% hull enrage sequence. At exactly 50% hull or below during advancing combat, immediately switch its looping Heart of Corruption opening to Lappy's The Savor of Tomorrow, reusing the existing Ungaikyo audio asset. The switch is irreversible for that battle, retries once per cue, and returns music to the campaign afterward; ordinary Gautama reincarnations retain Heart of Corruption. At the start of enrage, triple only Budai's native maintained shroud pool and quadruple its normal and overloaded smoke counts, widening spread by 35% and extending smoke duration by 25%. Apply these per-ship multipliers once, retry if the native shroud is not yet available, and retain native smoke textures, colors, movement, and cleanup.
- Battles against the actual Budai flagship loop **Heart of Corruption** by Lappy through `BudaiBattleCreationPlugin`; Gautama's own live hull also owns that cue outside the protected final-Labor soundtrack. Never apply it to ordinary Shrouded Dwellers or other THREAT ships without Gautama. Labor V's perimeter and center-breach combat retain their instrumental or vocal **My Gospel is Gunpowder** cues. Always preserve explicit Lappy credit and the source URL in both the README and audio-source record when distributing the bundled track.
- Avici campaign music uses the vanilla Shrouded Dweller encounter cue `music_dweller_encounter_hostile`, looping on entry and resuming after combat. Store this exact cue in Avici's native `$musicSetId` so replacement music-player plugins honor it; do not restore the timed KARMA intro/A/B playlist or copy/override vanilla audio. Keep native encounter/location music priority and release the custom stream on leaving. Budai's combat soundtrack and other systems are unchanged. Retain inactive KARMA assets and zippy's source credit.
- Sanzu campaign music loops zippy's `Watcher of the Cycle -Scene02- (NoVox)` from the official June 2026 YouTube-members download. Assign it through Sanzu's native `$musicSetId`; do not restore the one-shot campaign script. Combat may take precedence, and Starsector's location music must resume automatically afterward. Runtime music assets must be real Ogg Vorbis. Preserve zippy's credit and the public source-video URL in both the README and audio-source record.
- Budai is a one-time vengeance-style hunter confined to Avici. Use a direct lookup of his owned fleet ID in Avici, a spawn flag, and the durable `$chief_navigator_budai_defeated_v1` flag; never respawn him. Record defeat when Budai is actually absent from the owned fleet's post-battle roster or the fleet receives `DESTROYED_BY_BATTLE`, not merely because the player disengaged. Retire the original fleet and its remaining escorts after battle/dialog/transition activity ends; always exclude player fleets. No legacy timer conversion, reward-based defeat inference, global recovery search, duplicate reconciliation, or old-variant repair is required. Budai tracks the player's exact live position only while both fleets are in Avici, otherwise returns to his lair near Kshaya, and cannot use jump points. Avici's fog must not slow him or break pursuit. The player-involved defeat queues one rules-authored salvage scene whose extraction grants exactly two `chief_navigator_mountable_heavy_adjudicator` weapons with Isa or one without her and commits `$chief_navigator_budai_heavy_adjudicators_granted_v1`. Show the consumed Onslaught Mk.I only as a temporary scene fleet-member visual and cut it apart completely; never create a persistent wreck or recoverable hull. The weapon clones vanilla `heavy_adjudicator` art, projectile, sound, firing behavior, ammo, OP cost, and combat statistics. Remove only the `SYSTEM` hint and `H_A_only` group restriction, use a 500,000-credit base value, and exclude it from blueprints, production, dealers, random drops, and salvage drops. Isa helps only when Hall of Triumph's durable recruitment flag has been observed, with no Hall class dependency. If Budai remains undefeated after Labor V, add his authored Dweller escort once; a defeated Budai never returns.
- Once the player first enters any Odyssey Sector system, begin the region's stranded-human timeline. Sanzu has no hostile Guard patrols, Starving Threat, or Budai. Completing Labor I causes one large, personally hostile **Persean League Survivors** fleet to arrive there, centered on a Pegasus and supported by a Champion, two Eagles, a Gryphon, Falcons, Eradicators, destroyers, and frigates. Give every hull D-mods and damaged CR, keep the Pegasus less degraded than its escorts, force the formation to intercept the player without formal League reputation impact, prevent it from leaving Sanzu, and never respawn it after defeat. Preserve a canonical fleet that already arrived in an older save; replace only the former procgen League population with this authored composition. Sanzu also supports at most two small Persean exploration patrols: each has one or two combat escorts plus one Shepherd, Wayfarer, or Buffalo, remains non-hostile, and cannot leave Sanzu. Completing Labor III causes one battered Hegemony expedition to arrive in Alpha Odyssey and establish its persistent forward base at `(-6500, 4800)`. Require the first three Labors—the four Guard Motherships, delivery and installation of four distinct Ithaca defense components, and the Drifting Wall—to be complete; the other two stations and components remain optional and recoverable. The expedition is mostly logistics hulls, with many frigates, several Enforcers, a few cruisers, and exactly one Legion XIV; that Legion remains battered but uniquely has the maximum three S-mods. It is personally hostile regardless of the player's formal Hegemony relationship, hunts the player in Alpha Odyssey, and demands 60 supplies plus 120 fuel "for the good of humanity." Payment grants fourteen days of safe passage from both Voss's expedition and all three Foxtrot Terminus patrols; refusal or inability to pay starts combat without a harmless pre-battle leave option. Neither outcome changes formal Hegemony reputation, and the expedition does not respawn after destruction. Maintain exactly three small, personally hostile Foxtrot Terminus patrols after the expedition arrives. They orbit aggressively, cannot leave Alpha Odyssey, ignore formal Hegemony relations, and cause no faction reputation loss outside an active tribute-grace period. Their forward base and patrol cordon obstruct the player's ability to leave the Orion Knot unmolested. Only after this expedition's persistent arrival flag is set may at most two Hegemony exile patrols appear in Sanzu. Voss confiscates supplies, arbitrarily recalculates rations in the name of collective need, and sends malcontents into dangerous, barren reaches of the Orion Knot. He then treats their meager returns and predictable failures as further offenses until he can exile them. Exile fleets contain two or three frigates with a 30% chance of one outdated Enforcer, carry one or two D-mods per hull at 32-59% CR, and remain confined to Sanzu. Each rolls a posture lasting four to nine days: 45% of the time it becomes personally hostile and aggressive without always-pursue behavior, and otherwise it becomes non-hostile and actively avoids the player. Both small Sanzu patrol populations use randomized 18-32 day replacement intervals and never exceed two live fleets apiece. Admiral Oren Voss is a communist satire/critique: he dresses armed confiscation in language of collective ownership, voluntary contribution, arbitrary ration calculations, and the **Good of humanity** while steadily losing his grip on reality. His recurring requisitions are intentionally one of the pressures that makes casual movement into and out of the Odyssey Sector difficult.
- After that expedition arrives, play one save-persistent command-conference scene. Voss naively attempts to assume control of the entire FOB Ithaca macrocomplex under a claim of Fourteenth Battlegroup continuity. Menelaus refuses and cites his standing strategic authority as **Strategos**; Captain Aias Kleon remains Ithaca's acting Base Commander, and Task Force Spartan aligns with Menelaus. Voss lashes out and calls Menelaus a machine, a core, and merely a tool. Menelaus loses his composure, answers exactly and emphatically **"I AM HUMAN!"**, and casts the Hegemony officer out of Ithaca's command channel. The officer is publicly humiliated, but his expedition remains at its Alpha Odyssey forward base and turns to armed requisitions and harassment of the Orion Knot approaches.
- Menelaus's displayed Task Force Spartan rank is **Strategos**, using the dedicated `chief_navigator_strategos` faction-rank id, and his non-administrative post is **Strategic Intelligence**. Reapply both whenever his persistent person is loaded so older saves migrate away from the former **Admiral / Base Commander** identity and vanilla colony-management options cannot pollute his Labors menu. Captain Aias Kleon owns and continuously reapplies Ithaca's **Base Commander** post instead.
- Do not add `module_no_status_bar` to Charybdis. That tag hides the large targeting outline, but it also suppresses the locked-target hull and flux readout; preserving actionable combat information takes priority. Starsector exposes these as one vanilla target-UI behavior, not two independent skin options.
- Every Starving Threat base hull installs only `chief_navigator_starving_threat`, a complete replacement for stock `threat_hullmod`. It extends the stock implementation and retains the shared Threat combat stat package, but removes stock's zero-profile multiplier, applies +200% sensor profile, marks the ship against stock reclamation, and replaces that death outcome with attack swarms. Never install both hullmods on one Starving hull. Gautama's skin removes the custom hullmod and its variant must not equip `fragment_swarm` or stock `threat_hullmod`.
- Scylla inherits from the dedicated `chief_navigator_scylla_base` hull. Its 570x555 sprite, `(285,199.5)` pivot, collision bounds, shield, engines, and weapon slots are all authored directly at 150% scale; do not reintroduce runtime sprite/spec scaling. The ordinary and Labor V `chief_navigator_scylla` skin has exactly twenty fighter bays and twenty repeated `chief_navigator_scylla_attack_swarm_wing` built-in wings, establishing a hard twenty-swarm live cap with an unlimited five-second carrier refit. The retired `chief_navigator_scylla_final` zero-bay skin remains only for data and old-save compatibility and is not the Devoured Reach encounter. Every historical wing contains one stock-derived Attack Swarm carrying `chief_navigator_scylla_fragile_swarm`: 25% hull, 150% hull and armor damage taken, no underlying-ship explosion or hulk. Preserve ordinary damageability, omit the former fragment-chain renderer, keep `chief_navigator_scylla_invincible_swarm` inert for save compatibility, and never make the stock `attack_swarm` hull globally invulnerable.
- Starving Threat keeps normal top speed but has 5x acceleration, deceleration, turn acceleration, and maximum turn rate, 0.67x hull, and 1.15x armor. On death the custom hullmod suppresses stock reclamation, launches attack swarms, and in ordinary unsuppressed battles may begin Gautama's one-per-battle THREAT construction: 10% for a frigate, 20% for a destroyer, 30% for a cruiser, and 50% for the Fabricator. Starving Hive Units now use their native `TIMID` support personality and complete vanilla fit, geometry and Hive fragment reserve/regeneration bonuses; enforce the doctrine on campaign/fabricated Hives without changing other roles. This supersedes the former aggressive doctrine and Hive fragment exclusions.
- Avici contains no THREAT fleets or rogue-derelict flotillas; Budai is its only roaming hostile fleet. Ashen Verge maintains up to ten Ithaca-focused Starving Threat patrols, with a save-persistent three-campaign-day replacement delay after each defeat. It also materializes one transient First or Second Strike 11,000–15,000 units from Ithaca every 25–35 campaign days; this raid attacks one surviving bastion through Nexerelin's hidden-target station-assault sequence (`ATTACK_LOCATION` on an invisible 100-unit orbit token, then `GO_TO_LOCATION`, then `INTERCEPT`) and keeps the real bastion as its tactical priority target. It remains mutually hostile with the player so the player can join Ithaca's defense, and disappears after a twenty-day attack window so a survivor cannot block later monthly raids. The `SpawnSTInvasionFleets` console command is a test-only exception: it creates three full Third Strike fleets in Ashen Verge, assigning one persistent Nex-style assault to each of Ithaca's north, east, and south bastions outside Labor V. Command-spawned invasion ships and the canonical final-Labor invaders carry a hidden compatibility marker used only for battle-side discovery; it changes no combat statistics. Devoured Reach maintains twenty-four renewable three-group feeding masses: sixteen orbit aggressively against the ring and eight orbit the hollow worlds. None patrol the system or the hyperspace side, and its sole Threshold maintains a clear arrival bubble. Labor V's four-wave invasion remains inside Ashen Verge. Its first three fleets hold at separate standoff tokens instead of entering campaign autoresolve, cannot damage their bastions before their individual 60-day deadlines, permanently destroy those bastions at expiry, and then attack the north mouth guard, gate watch, and south mouth guard respectively (falling back to another surviving TFS fleet). Do not replace or repair TFS ships during Labor V. Count a perimeter wave only when its invasion fleet is actually destroyed, regardless of whether the player or TFS wins the battle. Spawn the Gautama center fleet only after all three perimeter fleets are dead, give it thirty-three supporting hulls in three staged Fabricator/Hive/escort waves, and teleport every surviving TFS formation directly onto it exactly once. Destroying the center fleet must set a durable return-to-Menelaus state, immediately stop Labor V's invasion controller, and keep the fifth Labor and Ithaca gate incomplete until the player finishes Menelaus's FOB Ithaca debrief. Closing that debrief queues a separate, one-time Hall-style 480×300 illustrated completion interaction; open it only after every other dialog, menu, and core tab has closed, and persist both pending and shown flags. Apply `MEMORY_KEY_NO_JUMP` to every THREAT fleet. Starving Threat fleets are manually populated autonomous swarms: disable their fleet inflater, migrate non-Ashen fleets and their serialized commanders from Omega to vanilla Threat identity in place, keep Ashen fleets on the dedicated Starving faction with Threat portrait and presentation, clear patrol status, ignore player comms, and use Threat-derived no-comm fleet interaction so they never show human patrol dialogue. Preserve existing fleet, member, officer, and skill objects during this migration. On load and each population update, delete non-quest THREAT fleets found in hyperspace and return Gautama to the Devoured Ring except while Labor V's center-breach fleet is active in Ashen Verge. Never recreate a defeated Ashen patrol on the next update.
- Command-siege persistence must compensate for campaign autoresolve, which does not run combat damage listeners. If autoresolve eliminates a `SpawnSTInvasionFleets` lane before the player participates, re-form its fleet and resume the same bastion assault. Once the player participates in that lane's battle, disable replacement there so the player's attrition and victory remain permanent.
- Each command invasion is exclusively locked to its assigned north/east/south bastion. Continuously set both the tactical priority and active target to that station, tell the invasion AI not to attack the player, TFS fleets, other bastions, or any unrelated fleet, and reciprocally tell non-player fleets not to intercept the invasion. Preserve hostile relations so the player may still voluntarily join the bastion battle.
- Avici also contains the doomed bombarded world Kshaya and its orbital habitat, originally built for 52,000 people but reduced to 87 cannibal worshippers of Budai. Sinni insists on joining the evacuation delegation, guarded by the player's two best bodyguards, Wei and Yvan. A mother and her young son secretly defect; rescuing them triggers a cult ambush and reduces the habitat's population to 85 before any ambush casualties. The player may bombard the habitat or withdraw. If spared, the habitat is warm, pressurized, and empty on the next interaction, with its final record implying that Budai consumed everyone left aboard. Preserve the two rescued passengers as a global story flag rather than ordinary cargo crew. Historical internal IDs retain `naraka` solely for save compatibility; every player-facing name is Kshaya.
- Ashen Verge patrols use the dedicated chief_navigator_ithaca_threat faction: hostile to both the player and Domain Combat Guard. Explicit assignments normally intercept surviving Ithaca bastions; no blanket MAKE_AGGRESSIVE or ALWAYS_PURSUE flags. Outside 3,500 units from Ithaca only, a patrol may briefly redirect from its siege target when it sees the player within 700 units and has at least 1.3x the player fleet points: 10% chance per eligible check, at most once per three campaign days, lasting up to 0.2 days. Losing sight, exceeding the proximity limit, or entering the refuge immediately cancels pursuit and restores the bastion assignment. IthacaSupportInteraction allows the nearest available, player-friendly wall module hostile to the opponent to support player encounters from 2,500 units (edge-to-edge), without globally expanding other fleets' join range or moving the campaign station.
- Starving Threat's governing theme is ephemerality, short-livedness, and mortality. Its archived bone-weapon prototypes express this through ammunition, longevity, and self-damage drawbacks, but active Starving fleets now use unmodified stock THREAT weapons. Do not restore the prototype Skullblaster or its self-damaging feedback to active loadouts.
- Starvation makes Starving Threat hulls ravenous and unconcerned with stealth. Encode this on the replacement `chief_navigator_starving_threat` hullmod itself as a +200% sensor-profile modifier (3x total profile), after explicitly removing the inherited stock zero-profile modifier, so fleet visibility scales naturally with its Starving hull composition. Do not use a fixed fleet-level visibility override or apply the bonus to Gautama and ordinary THREAT hulls.
- Starving Threat recovers zero supplies and uses stock THREAT weapon IDs. The inactive `chief_navigator_bone_*` definitions and `tools/generate_starving_threat_weapons.ps1` remain archived prototype material; generating those definitions must not put them back into fleet variants. Their historical half-OP costs and drawbacks do not apply to the active stock weapons.
- Non-Hive ordinary Starving Threat loadouts must not equip fragment-dependent weapons (`swarm_launcher`, `seeker_fragment`, `kinetic_fragments`, `unstable_fragment`, `devouring_swarm`, or `voltaic_discharge`). Their shared fragment economy is deliberately excluded as too complicated to balance for this faction variant. Gautama's fixed boss loadouts remain the authored exception.
- The Starving Fabricator uses `chief_navigator_starving_construction_swarm`, maps every constructable stock THREAT variant to its Starving counterpart, and subtracts half the normal construction CR cost from the Fabricator itself. Spawned units keep normal starting CR; do not apply a 50% CR penalty to them.
- Waypoint Troy has a separate persistent departure entity, `chief_navigator_troy_anchorage`, backed by an unlocked storage submarket. Departure must start only through `TroyDepartureInteraction`, never merely because the player entered the system. Sum active non-fighter fleet members' live deployment-point costs and block wormhole transit above 50 DP in both directions; recheck at the Troy entrance and Foxtrot Terminus Aperture at the instant of transition so storage transfers cannot leave a stale permission state. Leaving either custom wormhole without transiting must mirror vanilla jump-point exit handling: set campaign-UI following-direct-command state, unpause the sector, then dismiss the dialog so an active plotted course cannot immediately retarget the aperture.
- Never use `createSectorMap` for the Orion Knot chart: the native widget clamps its viewport to vanilla hyperspace bounds and cannot show the Knot at its remote coordinates. The Adept of the Stars and Waves Intel embeds no chart. After arrival, Sinni's contact launches a full-size custom-dialog chart that reads the six real authored `StarSystemAPI` coordinates and normalizes their relative positions into local panel space. Match the user's vanilla sector-map reference with stock planet-spec map icons and their native UI colors, stock blue map-nebula atlas cells sampled read-only from the actual hyperspace terrain, and the restrained blue square grid. Keep cleaned names, inset labels, the north reference, broken plotted traversal legs, and the regional Abyss label. Cache only panel-visible cloud samples when opening the chart; never render the whole atlas or advance/edit terrain. Plot the fleet only while it is within the Knot using the same coordinate transform as the systems. This is a live bounds-independent UI renderer, not a static image and not another Intel entry.
- The abandoned low-tech station beside Old Milix is player-facing as the **Domain Derelict Battlestation**. Its rules-authored inspection follows physical evidence through the outer hull, habitation decks, and command archive: the Fourteenth methodically disarmed it, moved its stores and components into the Abyssal departure column, and evacuated the crew rather than losing the station in battle. The conclusion grants no salvage or mechanical reward. Preserve the stable entity ID and serialized token, and stop intercepting the derelict inspection after the same entity becomes the Hegemony Expeditionary Forward Base.
- Every authored mobile Derelict fleet uses the teal Domain Combat Guard skins, is confined to realspace, carries `MEMORY_KEY_NO_JUMP`, and permits ordinary recovery of disabled Guard hulls. Friendly restored-mothership and Ashen Verge patrols use the Domain Combat Guard faction; hostile unmoored Labor roamers use the vanilla Derelict faction so they attack the restored patrols. The one-time Rogue Combat Guard flotillas retain their encounter-specific faction behavior. Migrate stock-colored Rampart, Bastillon, Berserker, Defender, Picket, Sentry, and Warden members in existing saves to their matching Combat Guard variants in place. Rogue flotillas never respawn after defeat and must never materialize within 7,000 units of the player. Their fixed anchors must remain clear of all four Guard Mothership Labor sites; migrate older serialized flotillas to the corrected layout once, but never move a fleet in battle, transition, or an active interaction. On load and population updates, relocate escaped legacy fleets from hyperspace to their assigned home system and remove duplicate or temporary combat-proxy fleets. Refresh native navigation avoidance around every in-system jump point for those four mobile families without clearing their assignments, tactical targets, or vanilla avoidance. Keep Rogue patrol anchors at least 7,500 units from every jump point by moving the existing hidden anchor token in place. If an intact mobile patrol enters the 4,500-unit jump-arrival danger radius, move that same serialized fleet beyond 6,500 units from every overlapping zone while preserving its roster, assignments, targets, orbiting state, and velocity; never delete or replace it to make the entry safe, never move a fleet in battle, transition, fixed orbit, or an active interaction, and do not reposition a fleet already outside the danger radius.
- FOB Ithaca's central entity must own no persistent `MarketAPI`. On loading a save from the retired market-backed implementation, move all storage cargo and mothballed ships back into the station's entity cargo, detach the authored residents, remove procedural market NPCs, unregister `chief_navigator_fob_ithaca_storage_market` from the economy, clear the station market, and enable free transfer without loss. The native Comm Directory may use only an unregistered carrier market temporarily attached for the duration of its constructor call and detached immediately afterward. The one-time approach scene must flow directly into the full Menelaus station introduction and then end; it must not open a reusable Labor hub. If an existing or interrupted save has completed the approach without completing the introduction, the first direct interaction with Ithaca's central station must supply that introduction as a fallback. Only after the introduction should the custom station interaction own Ithaca's storage, fleet transfer, refit, and native comm-directory entry point. Populate that directory in authored order with Menelaus, Captain Aias Kleon, and rescued residents; never include Sinni. The Kshaya rescue permanently adds Mira Sen and her son Toma as authored residents, with Mira as the directory contact; their dialogue remembers Sara only when she died during the rescue. Every later Menelaus briefing, report, Labor hub, upgrade, and free-repair dialogue is entered through **Discuss the Labors.** on Menelaus's contact; his dialogue must never offer recursive station-directory or station-services options, and leaving it returns to the native directory. The Odyssey Sector maintains sparse, one-time hostile Rogue Combat Guard Flotillas only in the Odyssey Expanse, Ashen Verge, and Last Light; Sanzu, Avici, and Devoured Reach contain none. Unlike boss and Starving populations, Combat Guard fleets must not set `MEMORY_KEY_NO_SHIP_RECOVERY`, because their disabled hulls are an important finite source of replacements while the expedition is isolated.
- The Odyssey Expanse, Sanzu, and Ashen Verge each contain exactly two fixed vanilla `station_research_remnant` salvage POIs. Generate them once per system behind `$chief_navigator_odyssey_research_stations_v1`; the marker must remain set after salvage so loading or re-entering the system never recreates their loot.
- Populate every ordinary Odyssey destination with dead worlds, frequent ruins, and one-time salvageable orbital-habitat and mining-station remnants. Ashen Verge is the densest graveyard: all seven of its authored worlds have ruins and it contains nine non-research salvage sites. Sanzu retains exactly its three flow-obstacle planets; add ruins to Cairn and Undertow instead of adding more worlds there. Keep Kshaya Habitat separate from Avici's generic remnants. Devoured Reach must contain no ordinary worlds, habitats, or mining stations—only Mara, Vey, Ketu, the Devoured Ring, and its Threat population.
- Odyssey Sector research stations never drop vanilla or custom AI cores. Remove their default `ai_cores*` salvage groups, strip legacy guaranteed `chief_navigator_odyssey_combat_core` cargo from surviving stations, and provide only the deterministic Ithaca defense component in addition to their ordinary non-core salvage. Guarantee all six authored stations by reconciling every unsalvaged station ID independently; the per-system generation marker must never suppress repair of one missing station. Record vanilla salvage with a durable per-station flag rather than inferring completion from an absent entity. Every surviving station remains permanently visible, labeled, and marked on its Tab system map, using a marker reason independent of the active Labor marker. Retain the obsolete core commodity, officer plugin, and skill definitions only so old saves deserialize safely; never register the campaign plugin that makes new copies assignable.
- Entering Waypoint Troy may announce and mark the anchorage, but must never lay in a course, enable autopilot, or otherwise move the player's fleet toward it. The player approaches and interacts with the anchorage manually.
- The obsolete standalone hostile Task Force Spartan test fleet no longer spawns in Alpha Odyssey/Odyssey Expanse. On load and during population reconciliation, remove only fleets bearing its historical `chief_navigator_task_force_spartan_test` ID or marker, deferring removal while one is in battle, hyperspace transition, or an active interaction. Preserve FOB Ithaca's three Spartan cordon fleets; they use separate IDs and are the story presence of Task Force Spartan. Retain the historical sample ID, marker, and battle plugin only for old-save cleanup and an already-running encounter.
- The eventual TFS story encounter supports either a player victory or defeat. Both branches must end in peaceful de-escalation rather than exterminating Spartan or establishing permanent hostility; combat outcome changes the framing of the reconciliation, not whether reconciliation occurs.
- The Spartan Phantom deploys one built-in two-craft `chief_navigator_boarding_pod_wing`. These are real fighters, not missile projectiles: their custom AI holds a close defensive orbit around the source Phantom and commits only after detecting an enemy hull not covered by an active shield arc. On contact the fighter becomes an attached shuttle rendered with the direct-render `-90` degree correction, follows the target's local armor position, displays `Boarded!`, and drains 0.01 CR per second for exactly 10 seconds. The deployed and attached representations both preserve the source shuttle's 18:22 aspect and off-center 9,9 pivot (scaled to 36x44 and 18,18). Resolve the impact by tracing `ShipAPI.isPointInBounds()` from the pod toward the target and refining the first outside-to-inside crossing; never attach at `getCollisionRadius()`, which is only a broad-phase circle and floats away from long/narrow sprites. Boarding is explicitly registered as a fighter loss, and the bay replaces pods on a 15-second refit cycle.
